# PROGRESS.md — Lego Loco Reverse Engineering Progress

> Durable cross-session record. Update after each significant milestone.
> See AGENTS.md § "PROGRESS.md" for usage conventions.

## Project overview

Reverse-engineering Lego Loco (loco.exe, 1998, MSVC x86) into idiomatic C++.
The Ghidra database is the single source of truth; all code derives from assembly.

**Binary**: `lego-loco-unpacked/Exe/loco.exe` (PE32, ~2,001 functions)

---

## Completed milestones

### Reconstruction and host runtime

- [x] **Foundation (2026-07-23–25)** — created/analyzed the canonical Ghidra database for `loco.exe`, established the subsystem source tree and shared compatibility types, reconstructed the foundational `GameObject`/`Entity`/`Building`/`BuildingMgr`/`Train`/`CGWND`/`Game` classes, and brought the SDL-hosted executable through its first complete launch/message-loop/clean-exit sequence.
- [x] **Assembly validation and C++ cleanup** — validated central class layouts, vtables, callers, signedness, allocations, and control flow; removed the initial literal-vtable, raw-`this` arithmetic, deleting-destructor, linkage, and `_Ctor`/`_Dtor` anti-patterns from the reconstructed core.
- [x] **SDL presentation/resource foundation** — implemented SDL3 DirectDraw/DirectSound/window boundaries, RFH/RFD archive and PE string-table loading, BMP/DAT/WAV/BUT/ANI resource access, primary-surface presentation, menu/lobby composition, intro-video playback, input routing, and archive-backed sound playback.
- [x] **Host menu and multiplayer flows** — reconstructed main-menu/player-name/lobby controls and feedback, added SDL_net transport/framing/handshake/routing, typed game-facing packet/session/Vehicle ownership, Direct Connect, discovery runtime, embedded mDNS, and Avahi D-Bus backends.
- [x] **Deterministic GUI testing** — added isolated Wayland/Sway integration flows, screenshots/log artifacts, host event instrumentation, and regressions for launch, menu, name entry, lobby, networking, mode-3 transition/input, and crash-sensitive click paths.

### Build, tooling, and quality gates

- [x] **Unified Meson source tree (2026-08-05)** — flattened the legacy split trees, retired source exclusion lists and Makefiles, colocated guarded host shims with their subsystems, ported unit/integration/linkage/network tests, and added the MinGW typecheck target.
- [x] **Autonomous RE tooling** — built the evidence-led task scheduler/daemon, SQLite event/evidence store, Ghidra adapter, approval/write-scope workflow, React graph dashboard, watchdog/recovery behavior, and dependency-driven task advancement.
- [x] **Strict compiler cleanup (2026-08-09)** — brought the native tree to a clean `-Dstrict=2` build, including old-style casts, missing declarations, strict null/cast diagnostics, and host/test class hygiene.
- [x] **LINK-001 closure (2026-08-11)** — removed duplicate-definition masking, fixed symbol/signature collisions, and reduced direct `call 0` landmines from 232 to zero with object/disassembly verification.
- [x] **Hardcoded allocation/FUN_ sweeps (2026-08-11)** — audited original x86 allocation literals against host concrete types and resolved the remaining Ghidra `FUN_` functions; unresolved model-dependent findings are listed under Remaining work.
- [x] **Fixed a real host OOM landmine in `persistence_adapter_test` (2026-08-13)** — `dmesg` showed the same ~4.2 GiB process getting OOM-killed 4 times over the past 5 days (`persistence_ada[pter_test]`), sometimes taking unrelated processes (`ssh-agent`, `dbus-broker`, `systemd`) down with it on this no-swap host. Root cause: the test's `make_doc()` helper unconditionally does `preview.assign(w * h, 0x05)`, and the "oversized preview dimensions" test case called it with `w=h=0xFFFF` (`0xFFFF * 0xFFFF` ≈ 4.1 GiB) purely to build a document meant to prove `write_save` *refuses* it — `PersistenceAdapter::write_save`/`check_layout` only ever read `header.player_id * player_color`, never `doc.preview.size()`, so the huge buffer was pure test waste, not exercised production behavior. Built that one document by hand (header fields only, empty `preview`) instead of routing it through `make_doc()`. Peak RSS for the test measured before/after: ~4.1 GiB → 12 MB. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`.

- [x] **EditWindow/UI inheritance** — integrated inherited `UI_WindowBase` dispatch, proved `ChildWindow` is a genuine base class, converted `CursorEditWindow`/`TrainStation`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor` to real derived classes, implemented their render paths, and removed a falsely attributed EH cleanup helper.
- [x] **Stream path recovery** — modeled the stream hierarchy and implemented the reachable `WIN32_StreamOpen`/file/path/read/destroy-immediate path without copying MSVC vbtable mechanics; the real mode-3 click regression passes.
- [x] **Mode-3 input and placement (2026-08-09–12)** — reconstructed `MainWndProc` mouse dispatch, implemented `INPUT_PlaceObject` and collection insertion, parsed physical/bitmap occupancy and tile classification metadata, added host resource adapters, and placed 4/497 real save records into actual tilemap slots through `TileMap::FindObject`/`ScrollRect`.
- [x] **Semantic C++ reconstruction standard (2026-08-13)** — made model-first canonical classes, inheritance, typed ownership, and virtual dispatch mandatory; versioned the reverse-engineering agent/workflow prompts and rejected cast-backed decompiler-shaped implementations.
- [x] **Mode-3 presentation host-safety + typed dirty-rect list (2026-08-13)** — made `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects`/`ProcessRect` host-safe end-to-end (resource landmine, cursor/primary-surface, child-window reads) and replaced the implicit dirty-rect linked list's truncating x86 `LONG` "next" slot with a real `DirtyRectNode` type. Flipping `ATTR_0047f108` still blocks on the separate, larger "wire the real `UI_Manager`" item below.
- [x] **`WIN32_StreamOpenWriteFile` reconstructed (2026-08-13)** — decompiled 0x465090 (Ghidra-mislabeled `CRT_floor`) and found it does NOT construct a `WIN32_Stream` at a different most-derived-construction level, despite `WIN32_StreamOpenFile`'s sibling ctor looking almost identical: its vbtable (0x479244) is a distinct, 4-bytes-smaller table `[self=0, vbase=0x8]` vs. `WIN32_Stream`'s `[self=0, vbase=0xC]`, confirmed independently via allocation size (`operator_new(0x58)` at the sole real caller, `RESMGR_LoadResourceData` 0x447E63, vs. `WIN32_Stream`'s documented 0x5C) and via the write-side `AttachBuffer` (0x465A30) zeroing one fewer field than the read-side one. Modeled the real sibling hierarchy — `WNDPROC_OStream : virtual StreamObject` (`resources/WndProcOStream.h/.cpp`) and `WIN32_OStream : WNDPROC_OStream` (`resources/Win32OStream.h/.cpp`) — and implemented the real constructor/facade through it. Found and fixed two live bugs at the one real call site (`resources/ResDataSave.cpp`'s `#else`/Windows-only branch, never linked on host but compiled by the MinGW typecheck build): an undersized `operator_new(0x58)` literal (now `WIN32_OStream_Size()`) and a value-vs-address bug passing the literal address `0x479190` instead of the value stored there (`0x1A4`) as the shareMask argument — the identical bug exists at one more call site a few hundred lines up, flagged but not fixed (out of scope, tracked with the already-known `DAT_00479190` multi-declaration follow-up). Documented the whole vtable family in `shared/vtable_addrs.h`. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`.
- [x] **`WIN32_StreamDestroy` contract established + removed (2026-08-13)** — decompiled 0x463A80 directly (4 instructions) and found it does no cleanup at all: it is pure MSVC "walk down re-tagging each virtual-base vptr as the destructor chain unwinds" bookkeeping, byte-for-byte analogous to `WIN32_OStream_DtorVftableReset`/`WNDPROC_OStream_DtorVftableReset` (`resources/WndProcOStream.h`) — its real argument is `&stream->StreamObject_subobject` (`this+0xC`), and the real cleanup every one of its ~15 original callers performs immediately afterward is a *separate* function, `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` (0x464620), on the same pointer. That pairing is exactly `~WIN32_Stream()`'s compiler-generated-bookkeeping-plus-real-body shape, so implemented `StreamObject::~StreamObject()` for real (frees an owned `rdbuf`, tears down the lock, decrements/tears down the shared refcounted global critical section — `resources/StreamObject.h/.cpp`) and converted every resolvable real caller (`game/TrainStation.cpp`, `game/ScriptedObject.cpp`, `ui/HelpWnd.cpp`, `ui/CursorEditWindow.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`) from a raw placement-new buffer (several under-sized relative to `sizeof(WIN32_Stream)`, one indexing the wrong offset, one dereferencing a value instead of taking an address) to a real stack-allocated `WIN32_Stream` local with real C++ RAII construction/destruction — no caller-side offset arithmetic anywhere. Removed `WIN32_StreamDestroy` as a callable symbol entirely (header/impl/LINK-001 bridge in `shared/stubs_link001_integration.cpp`) once `nm` confirmed zero remaining references in the linked binary. Also fixed two bugs surfaced while wiring this: `WIN32_StreamRead`'s facade was downcasting to `WIN32_Stream*` when the real callee (`WNDPROC_Stream::Read`) is a base-class method also called through the separate `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory` heap-stream class (UB on that path, `resources/Win32Stream.cpp`); and `stubs/windows.h` declared this project's own `operator_new`/`GLOBAL_free` allocator hooks with C linkage inside its Win32-API `extern "C"` block, conflicting with the ~21 files that already (correctly) declare them with C++ linkage the moment both became visible in one TU. Removed dead/wrong-address `WIN32_StreamDestroy`/`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup`/etc. declarations with no live call from `input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.cpp`, `ui/AboutDialog.cpp` (that file's `LoadCredits()` is still unimplemented), `resources/ResourceManager.cpp`, and `world/scriptengine.cpp` (the latter two cited real-looking addresses — 0x461600-series/0x4617C0/0x460D50 — that Ghidra decompile proved belong to unrelated functions, `WIN32_QueueAsyncTask`/`WIN32_SendNetworkData`). `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile`/`UI_ChildWindow_Create` are real original callers left unresolved because they are themselves not yet implemented (tracked in Remaining work). Note: the dispatch prompt's premise that 0x463A80's real body "presumably closes/cleans up via the StreamObject virtual base" was factually wrong — the cleanup is the separate 0x464620 — corrected via direct disassembly, not assumed. Advisor review after the first pass caught three more real issues, all fixed: `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`'s cleanup tail had a stray `WIN32_StreamDestroyImmediate(stream)` branch with no counterpart in the disassembly, calling `WIN32_Stream::CloseNow()` on the already-destroyed `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory` heap object (use-after-free plus the same over-narrow downcast just fixed elsewhere) — removed; `resources/StreamObject.h` was left at `// Status: TRANSCRIBED` despite gaining a real, disassembly-validated constructor/destructor — bumped to `VALIDATED`; and `shared/vtable_addrs.h` was missing the vtable address `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` pokes (`VTBL_STREAMOBJECT_ALONE`, 0x47922C) — added. Also found and fixed two live, now-more-undersized `operator_new(0x5C)` literals feeding real `WIN32_Stream` constructors in `native/wave_io.c`/`native/cgwnd_palette.c` (both reachable from real render paths — `CGWND_ValidatePaletteData`, called from the now-RAII-converted `CursorEditWindow::Render`) — `StreamObject` gaining a real vptr widened `sizeof(WIN32_Stream)` further past the original x86's 0x5C, an existing gap this change made larger; fixed both to use `WIN32_Stream_Size()`, left the sibling `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory`-class allocations in the same two files flagged but unfixed (no `*_Size()` helper exists for that still-unmodeled class). `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`, checked after every file, including after the advisor-review fixes. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: no file went from clean to broken; the only touched files with any error-set change already failed before this session (pre-existing `uint`/DirectX-typing breakage this session didn't introduce), and `stubs/windows.h`'s conflicting-declaration fix actually removed two pre-existing errors there.
- [x] **`WIN32_StreamClose` removed as compiler-generated EH-unwind-only dead code (2026-08-13)** — following the advisor's pick after the `WIN32_StreamDestroy` work, disassembled 0x463A60 directly and found the exact same two-call shape already resolved there: a call into `WNDPROC_Stream_DtorVftableReset` (pure vptr-retag bookkeeping) followed by a call to `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` (`StreamObject::~StreamObject()`, now real). `get_xrefs_to(0x463A60)` confirmed its only real caller in the original binary is a compiler-generated SEH unwind funclet cleaning up an in-progress `WIN32_StreamMem_Ctor` on exception — not game logic — and this codebase has zero real callers of it anywhere (confirmed via `grep`). The address `resources/Win32StreamMem.cpp` called `WNDPROC_StreamPutChar` under a "TODO: decompile" stub was not a distinct undecompiled function at all: it was 0x4648E0, the exact same address already fully resolved this session as `WNDPROC_Stream_DtorVftableReset` — the file's own comment had simply never been reconciled against the newer finding. Removed `WIN32_StreamClose`, the stale `WNDPROC_StreamPutChar` stub, and both now-fully-dead `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` no-op stubs (one C-linkage in `shared/link_stubs.cpp`, one C++-linkage in `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` — confirmed via `nm` to be two genuinely distinct symbols, `WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` vs. `_Z21WNDPROC_StreamCleanupPv`, neither with any remaining caller after the removal). Also verified, per advisor review of the prior `WIN32_StreamDestroy` work: `sizeof(WIN32_Stream)`/`sizeof(WIN32_OStream)` are still 128 (0x80) after `StreamObject::~StreamObject()` became virtual, matching `Win32Stream.h`'s already-documented value (no stale constant); and the `native/wave_io.c`/`native/cgwnd_palette.c`/`ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` `WNDPROC_StreamFromMemory` undersized-allocation gaps are all behind `if (g_asset_mgr != NULL)`, and `g_asset_mgr` has exactly one definition in the tree (`nullptr`, never assigned elsewhere) — confirmed dead, not a live heap-overflow risk, and documented as such rather than left ambiguous. `nm` confirms `WIN32_StreamClose`/`WNDPROC_StreamPutChar`/`WNDPROC_StreamCleanup` no longer exist in the linked binary. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration` (one `persistence-adapter` timeout on an unrelated, narrowly-linked test was confirmed a flake — passed directly in 6s standalone and on immediate re-run of the full suite). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: zero errors in any touched file.
- [x] **Wire the real `UI_Manager` singleton (2026-08-13)** — found the missing construction site: `get_xrefs_to` on the ctor (0x4238C0) showed its one real caller at 0x45C685, inside an anonymous thunk (0x45C680-0x45C697, now named `CRT_StaticInit_UIManager`) that Ghidra had never defined as a function; that thunk is itself listed as *data* in the MSVC CRT's `.CRT$XCU` static-initializer pointer table (0x47E020, part of the array at 0x47E000) — i.e. `UI_Manager::UI_Manager()` runs before `WinMain`, directly on the global storage at 0x4FD220 (which is the object itself in the original binary, not a pointer to one), with no explicit call from any game function. `ui/UI_Utils.h`'s "Called by: CGWND_InitAllSubsystems" doc comment was stale/wrong — corrected. Teardown is symmetric: a second thunk (0x45C6A0, now `CRT_StaticAtexit_UIManager_Reset`) is registered with the CRT's atexit-equivalent table (0x468170/0x4680E0 — Ghidra's FLIRT-based `_ungetwc_*` names for those are false positives; decompiling confirmed real atexit-table-growing behavior) and tail-jumps to `UI_ResetWindow`/`UI_Manager::reset()` (0x4239E0). The faithful host reconstruction needed no explicit `new`/`delete` call anywhere: `graphics/DDRAW.cpp` now defines a real `static UI_Manager g_tooltip_mgr_instance;` with `g_tooltip_mgr` (`void*`) pointing at it, so the compiler performs the same "construct before main, destroy at exit" sequence the original CRT table did — not a host-only deviation (no `#ifndef _WIN32`), since the mechanism is identical on both platforms. This forced two more fixes to be load-bearing rather than latent: `UITimerList::Resize` (vtable slot the ctor calls 3×) was delegating to `Timer_Resize`, a `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` stub that unconditionally asserts — implemented its real body (0x435D10 disassembly: grow/shrink `items`, trim trailing NULLs, zero-fill, preserve overlap, free old array) directly as the typed method instead; and `tests/host_mode3_bootstrap_test`'s narrow object-only link needed `obj_ui_utils` plus a local `operator_new`/`GLOBAL_free` fixture (same precedent as its existing `g_empty_string` fixture) since the real singleton construction now unconditionally runs at process startup in every binary that links `DDRAW.cpp`. Unblocks `ATTR_0047f108` (dirty-rect flip) and `INPUT_CheckScheduledEvents`'s deferred `UI_CreateMessageBox` call — both left exactly as-is, tracked separately. **Scope boundary**: this is construction only — `g_tooltip_mgr` stays `void*` deliberately (retyping it to `UI_Manager*` piecemeal while ~15 TUs still declare `extern void*` would itself be the extern-type-mismatch landmine class), and the facade free functions (`UI_HideTooltip`/`UI_CleanupTooltips`/`UI_DestroyTooltip`/`UI_ResetTooltips`/`UI_CreateTooltip`) are still inert stubs, not routed to the new real object; that, plus the `&g_tooltip_mgr`-as-object-pointer hazard those stubs currently mask, is re-opened as its own Priority-2 bullet below. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration` (two full clean runs at this file state; a concurrent, unrelated agent's in-progress edit to `resources/WndProcOStream.h` — never touched by this change — left the tree unbuildable as of the last check, independent of anything here: `graphics/DDRAW.cpp`/`ui/UI_Utils.cpp` each pass `g++ -fsyntax-only` cleanly in isolation).
- [x] **`Town::render_selection`/`deselect_building`/`update_selection`/`track_building`/`select_building`/`is_valid_placement`/`send_postcard` were misattributed to `Town` — moved to `GameView` (2026-08-13)** — closed both Priority-1 bullets above. Re-verified the disassembly evidence directly: `TileMap_ProcessRect` (0x456700) and `GameLoop_FrameUpdate` (0x45C3EA) load `ECX` with the bare immediate `0x4852A0` for all four original call sites, never a `Town`-pointer dereference; `RESDATA_BaseInit` (0x4544E0) keeps `ECX` unmodified through `CALL 0x00405790` (`Entity`'s own ctor), confirming `Panel : public Entity` (not `GameObject` directly, as `game/Panel.h` wrongly declared) — fixed `Panel.h`'s base class, removed its now-redundant `surface_ref`/duplicate `anim_index` fields (both are `Entity::resource`/`anim_index`, inherited), and fixed `Panel.cpp`'s one `surface_ref` reference. Fixed `GameView.h`'s hierarchy comment (`GameObject → Entity → Panel → GameView`). Investigating `track_building`'s own disassembly (not just its already-reconstructed-but-wrongly-attributed C++) surfaced a materially bigger fix than the two bullets described: `select_building` (0x42D040) is a forced dependency (`track_building` calls it with `this` unchanged, and `TileMap_HandleClick` loads the same `0x4852A0` immediate before calling it) and moved too, along with its only dependency `is_valid_placement` (static, no receiver). Reading `GameView`'s real vtable bytes (0x477D30..0x477D88, the true ceiling — Town's own unrelated vtable) found **22 slots, not the previously-documented 20**; slot [20] (+0x50) is `0x42D770`, reached *only* from `track_building`'s own child loop (zero other xrefs in the whole binary) — Ghidra's `Town_SendPostcard` label was a `Town`-family misnomer inherited from an unrelated address-adjacent naming pattern, not evidence of postcard behavior; renamed `GameView::update_cursor_child` and moved. `select_building`/`track_building`'s field accesses forced a further correction: `+0xE0` is `Building* selected_building` (a real pointer — `select_building` writes the actual `Building*` parameter there, `is_valid_placement`/`track_building` dereference it as one), not `int32_t scroll_x` as previously documented (unevidenced; the ctor's `=0` write is equally consistent with either). Also fixed the embedded-Entity misread the reconstructed-but-misattributed Town.cpp code had introduced: `MOV EDX,[this+0xE4]; LEA ECX,[this+0xE4]; CALL [EDX+4]` is `game_object_sub.InvalidateRect()` (embedded `Entity`'s own vtable[1]), not `this->child_panel->hide()` — there is no `child_panel` field on this class. `render_selection`'s previous `/* BUG: signature mismatch */` comment was itself wrong: the six pushed args are exactly `Entity::Draw(RECT, int, uint32_t)` as `core/Entity.h` already declares it. **Caught in review before commit**: the subagent's first pass wrote this as `this->Draw(rect, extra, 0)` — a virtual call — reasoning it was safe since no override existed between `Entity` and `GameView`. That's false: reading `Panel`'s real vtable bytes (0x4784C8) directly shows slot 11 (Entity::Draw's original position) holds `Panel::DispatchEvent` (0x454900), and decompiling 0x454900 confirms it's a genuine override (identical `(RECT, int, uint32_t)` signature) whose body calls `Entity::Draw` first and then dims overlapping child rects — behavior the original's raw, non-virtual `CALL 0x00405E60` deliberately skips for this one redraw. Fixed to `this->Entity::Draw(rect, extra, 0)` (explicit scope, bypassing virtual dispatch) to match. General lesson for this class of move: a raw hardcoded-address call in the original binary is sometimes deliberately avoiding a real override in between, not just historical vtable-slot reuse — check the intermediate classes' actual vtable bytes for a same-signature override before replacing a direct address call with a virtual one, don't assume "no override was documented yet" means "no override exists." Left `GameView::center_on_point` (0x42D440, vtable[3] — the real target of what the old code mis-called as a 4-arg `Town::set_mode`) as a declared, correctly-dispatched-to, loud stub (~550-byte scroll-deadzone algorithm, genuinely deferred — tracked in Remaining work) rather than guess at its body. Deliberately left `postcard_command_handler`/`handle_tile_click` on `Town` despite showing the same receiver evidence (tracked in Remaining work) — advisor-reviewed scope boundary. Wiring the six real methods up exposed that `Town_SelectBuilding`'s free-function bridge (used by `game/BuildingMgr.cpp`, `game/World.cpp`, `world/tilemap.cpp`, `world/scriptengine.cpp`, `town/sdl3_town_mode3.cpp`) was a **no-op stub in two places** (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp`) despite every caller already passing `g_town_view` — implemented both of the genuinely-distinct overloads that already exist across the tree (`void(void*, void*)` — pointer-safe, forwards to `GameView::select_building` directly; `int(void*, int)` — only two live non-zero callers, `world/tilemap.cpp:1740` and `game/World.cpp:913`, both truncate a real pointer through `int`, the same pointer-in-`int32_t` hazard `graphics/DDRAW.cpp`'s `DDRAW_SelectBuilding(void*,int)` bridge already documents and rejects loudly rather than corrupting on this 64-bit host — mirrored that exact, already-accepted pattern rather than inventing a new one). **This turns on real building selection across those four call sites** — a genuine behavior change, not just a header move; watch it first if anything regresses. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: zero new failures in any touched file.

- [x] **`Resource_IsValidTrackIndex`'s linkage fixed; `Resource_IsRoadTile`/`Resource_IsBuildingTile`'s dead stubs removed (2026-08-13)** — closes the follow-up flagged below the `WIN32_StreamDestroy`/"Stream tail" item. `Resource_IsValidTrackIndex`'s stub (`shared/link_stubs.cpp`) was `void`-returning while every real caller declared it `uint8_t`-returning — same symbol (return type isn't part of Itanium mangling), so every call read an uninitialized register. - [x] **`UI_Manager` tooltip facade wired for real; `&g_tooltip_mgr` address bug fixed (2026-08-13)** — closes this item. Fixed all nine `&g_tooltip_mgr`-vs-`g_tooltip_mgr` call sites (each callee independently re-verified via decompilation to confirm it really does expect the `UI_Manager` object, not a `UI_Manager**`) and retyped `g_tooltip_mgr` from `void*` to `UI_Manager*` across all ~17 real declarations tree-wide, in the same change as planned. - [x] **`UIPANEL_Surface::CalcScrollRect`/`CalcScrollRect_Reversed` implemented for real (0x42C590/0x42C700, 2026-08-13)** — closes this item. Ghidra's own 2-stack-arg signature guess was wrong (both take 4, matching `RET 0x10`); re-derived the real signature (`RECT* rect, IDirectDrawSurface4* surface_obj, RECT* clip_rect, uint32_t flags`) from raw disassembly of both functions and their sole caller, `UIPANEL_Blit`, rather than trusting the decompiler's `unaff_EBX`/`unaff_EBP`/`ptStack_4` artifacts (a direct consequence of the wrong signature it had synthesized). Both clip a scroll-view rect against the surface's `GetSurfaceDesc()` bounds, writing back into BOTH `rect` and `clip_rect` (genuine in/out params) — not mirror images: `CalcScrollRect` (forward/DDraw) validates `clip_rect` only against itself and its shared epilogue always returns false (apparent original bug, no observable effect since the sole caller never checks it); `CalcScrollRect_Reversed` (software) additionally clips against the panel's own logical bounds first, and returns true on success. Fixed the one caller (`UIPANEL_Blit`) to build and propagate two independent RECTs instead of the old wrong model's single rect that mixed unrelated fields (`src_x`/`src_y` from one pair, `clip_right`/`clip_bottom` from another). `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. MinGW typecheck: +2 errors for these two files (15→17), confined to an already-known, pre-existing `IDirectDrawSurface4`/`DDSURFACEDESC` incompleteness gap under the typecheck build only (not the real host build) — see the two new follow-up bullets below.

- [x] **`GameObject_BaseCtor` identified as `Entity::Entity`, misdeclared as a free function tree-wide; two size-safe call sites fixed (2026-08-14)** — 0x405790 is not an unimplemented Windows-side base constructor: it is `Entity::Entity(int resource_id, int16_t anim_idx, int world_x, int world_y)`, already implemented for real in `core/Entity.cpp`.   - **NOT fixed — gating size audit required first, tracked in Remaining work below**: the other 4 live call sites each target storage that is provably or plausibly smaller than host `sizeof(Entity)` (168 bytes) — the exact "undersized fixed buffer" landmine already documented for `ScriptedObject::sub_entity[0x88]` (its existing safe-default-stub precedent is why these were NOT mechanically wired the same way as the two above). Simply replacing the stub call with a real constructor call at any of these sites, without first resizing/retyping the target storage, would silently write past it into adjacent fields.

- [x] **`SpriteData` retired; `TileMap::asset_load_ptr`/`asset_enum_ptr` identified as real `AssetMgr*`; a live heap-corruption bug caught and fixed before commit (2026-08-14)** — closes the `SpriteData` ownership/layout item. `get_xrefs_to` on `SpriteData::SpriteData`/`~SpriteData` (0x45CDF0/0x45CE10) showed exactly 2 call sites each in the whole binary, both inside `TileMap::TileMap()`/`~TileMap()` — no independent `SpriteData` usage exists anywhere. `AssetMgr::UpdateAdjacencyGraph` reads the "resource_id" ctor parameter back via `(short)this->mode`, matching the real mode values (7, 8) passed at both call sites; the dtor's `CRT_malloc_zero`/`CRT_free` pairing on `pair_matrix` matches `UpdateAdjacencyGraph`'s own allocation of the same field. These are genuine `AssetMgr` objects, partially initialized (only `entry_count`/`pair_matrix`/`resource_array`/`mode` are written by the ctor; the constructor's C++ reconstruction zero-initializes the remaining 7 fields too — behaviorally identical to every real reader, since none reads them before `EnumerateCategory` freshly allocates into them, and strictly safer than replicating the original's accidental uninitialized-memory read). Moved the real ctor/dtor to `AssetMgr::AssetMgr(uint16_t)`/`~AssetMgr()` (`resources/AssetMgr.h`/`.cpp`), removed the `SpriteData` struct (`graphics/DDRAW.h`) and its now-dead `AssetMgr_ReadFile` free-function shim (its one caller is now a direct `ClearTree()` call inside the new destructor), retyped the two `TileMap` fields from `void*` to `AssetMgr*`, and added `AssetMgr_Construct`/`Destruct` bridges for `tilemap.cpp`'s pre-existing header-boundary conflict (same reason `AssetMgr_LoadFileEx`/`EnumFiles`/`EnumerateCategory` already stay free-function shims there). **Caught and fixed a live bug before committing**: the first pass kept `TileMap::TileMap()`'s original `operator_new(0x2C)` literal for the two allocations — but `AssetMgr` has 8 pointer-typed fields that widen from 4 to 8 bytes each on this 64-bit host, so `sizeof(AssetMgr)` is well past 0x2C; constructing a real `AssetMgr` into that undersized buffer corrupted the heap, caught immediately by `meson test`'s integration suite (glibc `malloc.c` assertion failure, every GUI test failing identically). This is the exact same "undersized fixed-size allocation on 64-bit host" landmine already documented for `network/DirectPlay.cpp`'s `shadow_mem` earlier this session — missed here because this pass's own focus was the type-*identity* question, not the allocation-*size* one; advisor's post-fix review flagged this as a generalizable rule (now recorded as a standing rule in Remaining work, not just a checklist for the unrelated `GameObject_BaseCtor` sites) and also verified two other risk points before signing off: the three `AssetMgr_LoadFileEx`/`EnumFiles`/`EnumerateCategory` free-function shims all `reinterpret_cast` and dispatch to real typed methods (no raw word-indexed field access survived from the pre-2026-08-09 conversion), and `EnumerateCategory` allocates and frees all 4 scratch fields unconditionally on its one exit path. Fixed with a new `AssetMgr_Size()` helper (returns the real host `sizeof(AssetMgr)`) used at both allocation sites instead of the literal. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration` (failed with the heap corruption before the size fix; clean after). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set (54/54) to the pre-change baseline via `git stash` diff.

Since that ambiguity feeds a `memcpy` destination/length directly, writing the function without resolving it first would be guessing at a memory-safety-relevant detail — deferred rather than guessed, per advisor's explicit call. **Correction to this tick's own earlier note**: the previously-recorded "`SoundResource`/`ResourceEntry` +0x120/+0x124 conflict" is not actually a blocker — neither `RESMGR_LoadSoundResource` nor `ReleaseSoundResource` touches `+0x124` anywhere in their decompiled bodies; both operate entirely within `ResourceEntry`'s already-unambiguous `+0x08/+0x0C/+0x18/+0x120` fields. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set (54/54) to the pre-change baseline via `git stash` diff; `nm` confirms zero dangling refs.

Confirmed via tree-wide grep that every real reader/writer (`graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`, `ui/GameWindow.cpp`, `town/TownTiles.cpp`) accesses these fields by name, not raw offset, so reordering the declaration changes no behavior — it just removes the latent trap for any future raw-offset reader. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set (54/54) — this file is `#ifndef _WIN32`-guarded, so unaffected by the typecheck build regardless.

- [x] **`UIPANEL_Surface`'s rule-of-three consolidated into real C++ construction/destruction/copy (2026-08-14)** — at the user's request to "get UIPanel_CreateSurface fixed properly". Disassembled all three real methods (`UIPANEL_CreateSurface` 0x42A110, `UIPANEL_DestroySurface` 0x42A140, `UIPANEL_CopySurface` 0x42A1C0) and every one of their 12+13 real xref callers directly rather than trusting the existing host transcription. Found the landmine was much larger than one function: 5 conflicting free-function definitions/overloads across `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `shared/link_stubs.cpp`, `shared/defsym_stubs.cpp` (a 0-arg no-op, a wrong-signature no-op, a differently-named no-op, plus `UIPANEL_CopySurface`'s own always-`nullptr` stub), each silently bound to by a different subset of ~13 caller files depending on which locally-declared, differently-shaped `extern` prototype happened to be in scope in that translation unit. Gave `UIPANEL_Surface` (`graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h/.cpp`) a real default constructor, virtual destructor, and deep-copy constructor (the copy ctor's `ddraw_surf`-duplication branch was disassembled precisely because the naive decompilation had the HRESULT success/failure branches backwards — `CMP EAX,ESI(0); JZ` fires on **success**, not failure — corrected via raw instruction trace, then implemented with `IDirectDraw4::CreateSurface`/`IDirectDrawSurface4::Blt` named virtual calls, no raw vtable dispatch); deleted copy-assignment (no assignment xref exists anywhere in the binary). Migrated every real call site (`game/BuildingPanel.cpp`, `input/Cursor.cpp`/`Cursor_new_impls.cpp`, `native/ddraw_init.c`, `network/Netman.cpp`, `network/NetworkPlayerList.cpp`, `resources/ResourceManager.cpp`, `town/Town.cpp`, `ui/AboutDialog.cpp`, `ui/EditWindow.cpp`, `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`, `ui/UI_ChildWindow.cpp`) from the `operator_new(0x20)`+null-check+free-function idiom to `new UIPANEL_Surface()`/`delete`/`new UIPANEL_Surface(*src)`, and retyped `g_thumbpal_surface` (DDRAW_Init's persistent thumbnail-palette surface) from `void*` to `UIPANEL_Surface*`. Found and fixed four collateral bugs surfaced along the way, each with its own field-offset evidence: `input/Cursor.h`'s `background_surface`/`toolbar_sprites` were mistyped `UIPANEL*`/`ButtonSprite*` (an unrelated widget class and an unrelated sprite class) with every reader compensating by reading through wrong-but-same-offset field names (`sprite->y`/`sprite->sourceX` for what is really `UIPANEL_Surface::width`/`height` at the identical +0x08/+0x0C, confirmed by the code's own "+0x08 width"-style comments already admitting it) — retyped both fields to `UIPANEL_Surface*` and fixed every reader to the real field names; `ui/ButtonSprite.h`'s `Sprite_Destroy` was wrongly declared `extern "C"` (conflicting with `network/Netman.h`'s and three other files' correct C++-linkage declarations the moment both became visible in one TU — the same landmine class as this session's other `extern "C"`-around-decompiled-C++ fixes), fixed there plus its two `extern "C"`-block duplicates in `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp`/`town/Town.cpp`/`native/NETMAN_NetworkUI.c`; `resources/ResourceManager.cpp` had a dead, wrong-address `UIPANEL_CreateSurface` declaration (cited 0x4286B0, which Ghidra confirms is actually `UIPANEL_DrawButton`) with zero call sites (`RESMGR_LoadResourceData`, the one real xref caller, is declared but never implemented in this tree) — removed rather than "fixed" since there's nothing to call yet; `town/Town.cpp` cannot include `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.h` directly (a separate, pre-existing landmine: it also defines a conflicting, differently-shaped `class PostcardAlbum` that collides with `ui/PostcardAlbum.h`, already documented on `town/Town.h`'s `UIPANEL_Surface` forward-decl and mirrored in `core/GameView.cpp`) — added one factory function, `UIPANEL_Surface_New()`, as the minimal, explicitly-documented bridge for exactly that case, keeping `Town.cpp`'s existing `UIPANEL_SurfaceView` field-read mirror rather than inventing a new workaround. **Verification methodology note**: this tree's native link uses `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all`, so deleting the dead stub definitions made several files "compile clean" while still referencing now-undefined symbols that would only crash at first runtime use — caught this via `nm -u`/fresh-touch-and-relink on each file rather than trusting a clean `meson compile`, and added a new regression test (`tests/uipanel_surface_construction_test.sh`, wired as `uipanel-surface-construction`) asserting by address that exactly one concrete ctor/dtor/copy-ctor exists and that no stale free-function reference remains, so this class of masked landmine can't silently regress again. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 including `--suite integration` (2 pre-existing unrelated network-namespace failures, `embedded-mdns-discovery`/`sdl3-net-discovery-transport`, confirmed present on the unmodified baseline too). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set (53/53) via `git stash` diff. **Explicitly not attempted**: the actual `Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected` blit adapter for host `SpriteResource` (Priority 3 finding 1 below) is a separate task, unaffected by this fix — real `UIPANEL_Surface` construction is now available for it to build on, but nothing wires host sprites through it yet.

## Remaining work

> Only actionable work belongs here. Completed investigations and superseded
> intermediate states are retained, if needed, in the compact milestone digest
> or the referenced source/design documents.

### Priority 1 — live correctness and object-model defects

- [ ] **Mode-3 town background/terrain is completely missing — needs a currently-nonexistent subsystem, not a bug fix (scoped 2026-08-14)**: confirmed no code anywhere in this tree loads or draws a per-town base terrain/background bitmap. `TileMap::ProcessRect`'s only two draw-shaped calls are gated on `obj != NULL` (confirmed identical in the original's own decompiled 0x456700 — empty tile slots draw nothing in the original either). `INPUT_LoadSaveFile`'s real decompile (0x41D5C0) shows the `.sav` header carries no background/map bitmap reference. `Town::init_postcard_sprites`'s `background_resource`/`background_surface` (+0x64C/+0x650) is the postcard-mailing UI's background art, unrelated to the town canvas — a checked and ruled-out red herring. Real shipped saves (`SAVEGAME/*.SAV`) place only a few hundred decorative objects onto grids far larger than that, so terrain must come from a separate, unrecovered mechanism — most likely a per-town static bitmap, or a `mode==0` software tile-cache initial paint (`UIPANEL_Blit`'s `DrawTile`/`InitTileCache` flag-dispatch path in `town/TownTiles.cpp`, never exercised on host since every host sprite adapter hardcodes `mode=1`). Needs the real function(s) that load/select and initially paint a town's canvas identified via Ghidra before any fix is attempted — likely a dedicated RE pass.
- [x] **Multi-frame sprites (track switches/points, and likely other animated buildings) rendered only their first animation state — real root cause found and fixed (2026-08-14), per user report of specific missing buildings and a switch/point "only renders pointing down, never up" bug**: user named concrete symptoms (repair station, three-chimney factory, tank, train station, post office missing; diverter/point tracks only render in one of their two states). Identified real resource IDs via a scratch dump of `loco.exe`'s `PeStringTable` (`building\factory3`=2074, `track\Depot-n/s/e/w`=3156-3162, `track\post`=3164, `track\pnt-*`=3082-3096 — 8 diagonal point/junction pieces, the actual "divider tracks", not `track\switch-h`/`swtch-vl`/`swtch-vr` which turned out unused in this save). Per-resource diagnostics showed depot/post/factory3/point pieces *were* being placed with valid, in-bounds `SetAnimState` calls (including both `anim_state=0` "closed" and `anim_state=1` "open" for the same point-resource IDs) — ruling out placement and animation-index-selection as the cause. Root cause: `Entity::InitBase`/`Entity::SetFrame` (`core/GameObject.cpp`) computed each frame's source-rect X offset as `frame_id * sprite_width(sprite)`, but `sprite_width()` returns the *entire decoded bitmap's* width — for any multi-frame sprite (a horizontal strip of `total_number_of_frames` equal-width frames, confirmed by `Entity::SetFrame`'s own linear-only indexing with no Y/row term), every frame past index 0 sampled from far outside the actual bitmap, landing on garbage/transparent pixels. Frame 0 always happened to work by coincidence (`0 * width = 0`), matching exactly why "closed"/first-state single-frame-equivalent sprites rendered fine while "open"/second-state (and presumably other >1-frame buildings) silently failed. Fixed by adding `loco::assets::sprite_frame_width()` (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.h/.cpp`): `bitmap_width / total_frames` (falls back to the whole width when `total_frames<=1`, i.e. no behavior change for single-frame sprites), used in place of `sprite_width()` at both call sites. Added the matching stub to `tests/persistence_fixtures.h` (a narrow-linking test fixture, same pattern as `sprite_width`/`sprite_height`). Verified visually and by pixel-diff: a fresh screenshot shows new building content (matching the point/junction pieces' second frame) that wasn't there before; `-metric MAE` moved from 0 (previous fix's no-op) to a real, non-noise value, and the diff image's highlighted regions align exactly with where the new content appears. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: no new errors (all remaining errors are the pre-existing `ushort`/`uint`/`offsetof`/`loco::host` baseline, unrelated to this change). Not yet re-checked: whether "repair station"/"tank" (not found in the string table by any obvious name — may be informal names for a resource this session didn't identify) and "train station"/"post office" (both confirmed placed and animated correctly — `Depot`/`post`, cells_written=49/54) are now visible on screen; a fresh screenshot comparison against the user's own screenshots is the natural next check.
- [x] **`world/tilemap.cpp`'s `g_player_id`/`g_player_color` globals were actually `TileMap::tile_count_x`/`tile_count_y` (fixed 2026-08-14)**: the file's own pre-existing extern comments already said "(TileMap.tile_count_x)"/"(TileMap.tile_count_y)", but 9 call sites (occupancy-bit indexing in `InvalidateRect`/`ScrollTo`/`InvalidateDirtyRects`/`ProcessRect`/`FindObject`, grid bounds checks in `ProcessObjectTimer`) still read the wrongly-shared network-player-identity global (defaults to 0 outside multiplayer), collapsing the row-stride occupancy formula to alias every row onto the same bit. Confirmed via Ghidra disassembly of `GAMESTATE_SendScenarioSelect` (0x40AD20) that the original binary genuinely reuses this storage for both roles. Fixed by reading `this->tile_count_x`/`tile_count_y` directly at all 9 sites. Real, evidenced, worth keeping — but pixel-diffed byte-identical (MAE=0) before/after, so **not** the cause of the missing-types symptom above.
- [x] **Two real render-coverage bugs found and fixed against the `wildwest-minimal.png` reference target (2026-08-14)**: user reported "the centering is visually correct, but a lot of buildings and rails are missing." (1) `TileMap::width`/`height`/`viewport_rect` are documented (`tilemap.h`) as *aliasing* the free-standing globals `g_world_width`/`g_world_height`/`g_viewport_rect_*` in the original — true only because the original `TileMap` singleton lives at a fixed address and these members sit at the exact global offsets; on host `TileMap` is a heap object, so the member writes in `TileMap::Init()` never touched the separate globals, and `shared/link_stubs.cpp` permanently hardcoded `g_viewport_rect_right/bottom = 800/600`. `TileMap_FreeDirtyRects` clips every pending dirty rect against these globals and silently drops any rect outside them — for the real 1280×1024 world this dropped most placed content outright. Fixed by explicitly syncing `g_world_width/g_world_height/g_viewport_rect_*` from `TileMap::Init()`'s computed `width`/`height`, host-only (`#ifndef _WIN32`, `world/tilemap.cpp`, `set_center:` block). (2) `TileMap::FindObject`'s (0x4550C0) two active-layer-byte update sites (`tile_data[(tile_index+2)*0x40-0x48]`, one in the physical_occupancy-grid loop, one in the bitmap_occupancy/span-map loop) compared the byte as `uint8_t <=`, but the byte is initialized to sentinel `0xFF` by `ResetWorldState` ("no layer written yet") and Ghidra disassembly of the real x86 (`TileMap_ProcessRect`/`FindObject`, `CMP AL,[EAX]; JL` at 0x455247) confirms the comparison is **signed** — as unsigned, 0xFF reads as 255 ("already the max"), so the byte could never advance off the sentinel; as signed it's -1 ("less than any real layer"), matching `ProcessRect`'s own signed read of the same byte. Fixed by casting both sides to `int8_t` at both sites. This specifically fixed multi-layer buildings (clocktower, vote-booth topper) rendering as the wrong/no sprite despite correct placement and dispatch. Verified with a rigorous coverage metric, not eyeballing: `magick <shot|ref> <arrid.bmp backdrop> -compose difference -composite -threshold 5% -colorspace Gray -alpha off` isolates real content from the sand background per image; coverage = (ref-mask ∩ shot-mask) / ref-mask via `-compose darken`. Went from a badly-degraded pre-fix render to **84.21%** content coverage against `tests/reference/wildwest-minimal.png` post-fix (`ref_content_px=225183`, `intersect_px=189636`). `meson test -C build`: 35/37 (2 pre-existing unrelated netns failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: no new failures introduced (pre-existing `uint`/`AssetMgr_LoadFileEx`/`Scroll` baseline errors at lines unrelated to these edits).

  **Dead end, recorded so it isn't retried**: while chasing the remaining gap, hypothesized that freshly-placed objects needed `TileMapObject::is_moving = 1` at construction (so `ScrollRect`'s blocker check would treat them as displaceable) and wrote it via `reinterpret_cast<TileMapObject*>(this)->is_moving = 1` in `ResourceGameObject`'s constructor (`core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`). Diagnostic trace showed the byte read back as 0 by the end of the same constructor chain. Initially misattributed this to colliding with `ResourceGameObject::group_active` at the same nominal +0xC0 — **that attribution was wrong, and the real explanation is worse; see the finding below.** Reverted the write and its diagnostics regardless (placement coverage was unaffected either way: 440/497 before and after) — a raw offset-cast write onto an unverified 64-bit layout is a CLAUDE.md anti-pattern independent of what it happens to hit.
  - **Landmine, not yet fixed**: `world/tilemap.cpp:53` declares `extern uint8_t g_is_fullscreen`, but its one real defining declaration (`shared/link_stubs.cpp:850`) is `int32_t g_is_fullscreen = 0`. A genuine cross-TU type mismatch (ODR violation); happens to read correctly today only because the value has stayed 0.
  - **The missing content IS the 57 `ScrollRect` rejections — confirmed by direct tile-coordinate correlation, not inferred (2026-08-14)**: added a temporary diagnostic logging every rejected record's `resource_id`/`tile_x`/`tile_y` from `TileMap::FindObject`, and separately extracted connected-component bounding boxes from the missing-content mask (ref-mask AND NOT shot-mask, from the coverage metric above). Converting screen pixels to tiles is a direct `/16` (`TileMap::Init` on this build produces `width=1280,height=1024`, exactly the screen/reference size, so `TileMap::ProcessRect`'s own `pixel = tile << 4` mapping has zero additional offset) — every single one of the 57 rejected records' tile coordinates falls inside one of the missing-mask's connected components; several of the largest components (48×48px = exactly 3×3 tiles) are individual rejected buildings, and the largest ones are clusters of several adjacent rejected records (e.g. a run of `paths\path`, resource 0x3000, plus several `building\*`/`track\pnt-*` records in the same area) that happened to merge into one blob after mask thresholding. Total rejected footprint area (~35,573px by tile count) matches the measured missing-pixel count (35,547) almost exactly. Resolved resource names via the `PeStringTable` dump (scratch `dump_strings` tool): the 57 rejections are a genuine mix of real content — `track\pnt-en`/`pnt-wn`/`pnt-ne` (switch/point pieces), `track\long-t-v`/`long-t-h` (turnouts), `track\strhlf-v`, `building\school`/`sk8-shp`/`piza-shp`/`gas`, `scenery\tree3`/`tree4`/`s-flower`, and `paths\path`/`statn2-e` — matching the user's original "buildings and rails missing" report exactly. **This rules out a `ProcessRect`/active-byte drawing gap for the remaining content**: nothing is placed-but-undrawn: it's rejected-before-placement.
  - **Root cause of the rejections found, and it's more serious than a missing initializer — confirmed by `sizeof`/`offsetof`, not inferred (2026-08-14)**: `ScrollRect`'s blocker check (`world/tilemap.cpp:1502`) and every `is_moving`/`tile_x`/`tile_y`/`occupancy_*`/`build_*` access tree-wide (`input/InputMgr.cpp`, `game/Building.cpp`, `game/World.cpp`, ~54 call sites) go through `TileMapObject*` — a hand-written x86-offset **mirror struct** (`world/tilemap.h`), `reinterpret_cast`ed onto whatever real, polymorphic C++ object (`ResourceGameObject`, `RESDATA_GameVehicle`, `Building`, plain `Entity`, …) was actually allocated there, per CLAUDE.md's forbidden "local `*View`/partial-layout struct over a known game object" pattern. `TileMapObject::resource` (a real pointer field at the mirror's +0x40) is 8 bytes on this 64-bit host, not the 4 bytes the original x86 layout assumed — but every field *after* it (`tile_x`/`tile_y`/`is_moving`/`occupancy_links`/…) is placed via a fixed-size `uint8_t _pad[]` array sized for the 4-byte original, so the whole back half of the struct is shifted 4 bytes from its intended position. Verified directly with a standalone `sizeof`/`offsetof` probe (scratchpad `offset_check.cpp`, compiled against the real headers): `offsetof(TileMapObject, is_moving) == 196` (not the documented 192/+0xC0), `offsetof(TileMapObject, tile_x) == 140` (not 136/+0x88), while `sizeof(Entity) == 168` — meaning `is_moving` is accessed **past the end of a plain `Entity` object entirely**, and for the larger derived classes that do extend that far (`sizeof(ResourceGameObject) == 344`, `sizeof(RESDATA_GameVehicle) == 360`) it lands on whatever real member each concrete class happens to declare at byte 196 — a different, unrelated field per concrete type, not a consistent "is_moving" flag at all. (This also retracts the earlier "collides with `group_active`" claim above: `offsetof(ResourceGameObject, group_active) == 264`, nowhere near 196 — there was no collision; the write landed somewhere else entirely, most likely inside `member_objects`/`linked_objects`/`fields_c4_e0`, which the constructor's own subsequent field-init lines then overwrite — consistent with the observed 1→0 clobber.) **This is a live, silent memory-corruption bug, not just an unreliable read**: every `obj->tile_x = …`/`obj->is_moving = 0` write through this mirror corrupts whatever real field of the actual concrete object occupies that byte range on this host. It explains why `ScrollRect`'s displaceable check never passes (the byte it reads is essentially never a real "moving" flag) and is very likely part of why the earlier `is_moving=1` diagnostic write behaved unpredictably.
  - **Scope call, per advisor review**: fixing this properly means giving `TileMapObject`'s fields (`tile_x`/`tile_y`/`is_moving`/`occupancy_links`/`occupancy_scores`/`occupancy_more`/`build_links`/`build_scores`/`build_more`, at minimum) a real, canonical home inside the actual `Entity`/`GameObject` hierarchy — real named members with compiler-assigned offsets, not a hand-offset mirror — and converting every one of the ~54 call sites above off `reinterpret_cast<TileMapObject*>`. That is a dedicated model-unification pass (same class of work as the already-tracked `ScriptedObject::sub_entity`/`DDRAW_Building` undersized-embedding items in this same Priority-1 section), not a quick follow-up to this thread, and was deliberately not attempted this session. Not fixing this is why coverage is capped at **84.21%**, not 90% — the fix is scoped, not started.
  - **RESOLVED (2026-08-14) — `TileMapObject` mirror struct deleted, real canonical home found and wired**: identified `ResourceGameObject` (`core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.h`, ctor 0x4580a0) as the one real class every `TileMap::FindObject`-placed object either is or chains through (`RESDATA_GameVehicle_Ctor` 0x44AE80 chains through this ctor as its base; `HelpWnd_FindPage` 0x44F210 and plain buildings/track/scenery all construct it directly) — confirmed `Building` (`game/Building.h`) is a distinct sibling class reusing +0x88 for unrelated fields, never written into the tile grid, so no collision. Added real named members at Ghidra-verified offsets (`group_active` +0xC0, `occupancy_links[4]`/`occupancy_scores[4]`/`occupancy_more` +0xC4..+0xE4, `build_links[4]`/`build_scores[4]`/`build_more` +0xE8..+0x108, all backed by `static_assert(offsetof(...))`), converted `occupancy_links`/`build_links` to real typed `ResourceGameObject*[4]` (previously raw `int32_t`, which would have reintroduced the same pointer-round-trip class of bug the tile-pointer registry already fixed elsewhere) and deleted `TileMapObject` outright. Converted every one of the ~54 call sites tree-wide off `reinterpret_cast<TileMapObject*>`/local `as_tilemap_object`/`as_entity` helper functions (`world/tilemap.h`/`.cpp`, `input/InputMgr.cpp`, `game/Building.cpp`) to direct typed member access or `static_cast<ResourceGameObject*>` (a genuine, well-defined downcast from `Entity*`, not a cast-around-uncertainty). `game/Vehicle.cpp`/`game/World.cpp` needed no changes — confirmed via grep that zero `TileMapObject` references existed in either file; `Vehicle`'s own `tile_x`/`tile_y` are genuinely separate fields on a standalone class, not conflated with the mirror struct as originally hypothesized. Root-cause-fixed `ScrollRect`'s displaceable-object check (`obj->is_moving` → `obj->group_active`, the field the ctor actually sets to 1) and a second, independently-verified `+0x06`→`+0x18` mistake in `ScrollTo` (disassembly of 0x455AB0 shows `CMP byte ptr [ECX+0x18],0x1`, i.e. `GameObject::initialized`, not a nonexistent `TileMapObject::object_state` at +0x06). One evidence gap honestly left unresolved rather than papered over: `Building::RemoveOccupant`'s `tile_entry` (reached via a *different* object's own `+0x20`/`+0x14` chain) could not be independently verified against the same ctor-chain evidence `ResourceGameObject` has, so it stays a documented raw offset read rather than being asserted as `ResourceGameObject*` — see that function's own comment.
  - **Fixing this exposed two more real, previously-dormant bugs on the same load path, both fixed** (this is the "some of the 57 rejections might have other independent causes" outcome the scoping note anticipated): (1) `INPUT_RemoveObject` (0x41DEF0) was mis-classified in an earlier session as "editor-only, not on the load/save path" and left as a loud `std::abort()` stub; `ScrollRect`'s fix made its displaceable-object check pass often enough that `ScrollTo`'s real body (also previously dead) now reaches it from the load path for the first time, confirmed via `coredumpctl` backtrace (`abort → INPUT_RemoveObject → TileMap::ScrollTo → TileMap::ScrollRect → TileMap::FindObject → INPUT_LoadSaveFile`). Fully decompiled and implemented it for real per the stub policy (collection removal, a scenery/track neighbour cascade via `TileMap_GetViewport`/`TileMap_ScrollTo`, optional removal message box, real `delete`). (2) The new `INPUT_RemoveObject` body's own scenery-classification helper initially called `RESDATA_IsSceneryTile(reinterpret_cast<intptr_t>(resource))` directly — the identical pointer-truncation landmine already fixed for `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile`/`RESDATA_IsRoadTile` elsewhere in this codebase (the real function takes `int32_t`, a genuine 64-bit host pointer does not survive that round-trip) — confirmed via a second `coredumpctl` backtrace (`is_host_sprite_resource → ResolveTileStateByte → RESDATA_IsSceneryTile → INPUT_RemoveObject`) and fixed with a local `is_scenery_tile` lambda mirroring the same `loco::assets::sprite_tile_type_byte` host-accessor pattern already established for `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile`'s host branch.
  - **Third crash found on the same load path, diagnosed and confirmed genuinely out of this item's scope, not fixed**: after both fixes above, the integration suite still failed the same way. `coredumpctl` (now with full symbols) traced it to `classify_tile_track_type` (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.cpp`) — a crash inside `std::string::size()` reached through `std::unordered_map<std::string, TileTrackType>::find`, called from `parse_sprite_metadata` → `ResourceManagerSdl3::get_sprite_by_id` → `ResourceManager_GetById` → `TileMap::FindObject`. This call site is `FindObject`'s *first* statement for a not-yet-cached resource ID — earlier in the call chain than any `ResourceGameObject`/`ScrollRect`/`ScrollTo`/`INPUT_RemoveObject` code runs on that same iteration, and none of this pass's field/member changes are on that path (`classify_tile_track_type`'s map keys are function-local `static const` strings, never touched by any `ResourceGameObject` write). Fixed with a Lock/Unlock-boundary adapter, not by touching either decompiled consumer: `Lock()` now converts the GPU-side XRGB8888 readback down to `SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGB565` before handing back `lpSurface`/`lPitch`; `Unlock()` converts the (possibly game-modified) 565 buffer back up to XRGB8888 before `SDL_UpdateTexture`. Chose RGB565 (not 555) as a fixed host display-mode choice — this host has no real display to query, unlike the original — and set `g_surface_bpp`/`g_surface_channel1`/`g_surface_channel2`/`g_surface_bshift`/`g_surface_red_mask`/`g_surface_blue_mask` (0x485274/0x485278/0x48527C/0x485280/0x485288/0x485290) to the exact values `DDRAW_GetSurface`'s own decompiled 565 branch uses (`0x235`/`0xb`/`6`/`0x7bef`/`0xF800`/`0x001F` — re-decompiled and confirmed via Ghidra, not guessed), inside `SDL3_EnsurePrimarySurface()` alongside the `g_ddraw` wiring. Added `tests/sdl3_ddraw_pixel_format_test.cpp`: confirms `Lock()`'s pitch is a genuine 16bpp stride and a raw `uint16_t` pixel write round-trips correctly through `Unlock()`+`Lock()`. `meson test -C build`: 34/34 (2 pre-existing unrelated failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set.
    **`ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` migrated off its shadow types (2026-08-14)**: removed the local, non-virtual shadow `DDSCAPS2`/`DDSURFACEDESC`/`IDirectDraw4`/`IDirectDrawSurface4`/`DDBLTFX` block (was lines 62-67) in favor of `#include "../platform/ddraw_interfaces.h"`; retyped `g_ddraw`/`g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer` externs to `void*` with corrected address comments; fixed `DDRAW_LoadBmpToSurface`'s forward declaration (was wrongly typed `HDC`, real return is `IDirectDrawSurface4*`); converted 3 `memset`-zeroed `DDSURFACEDESC`s to brace-init (unsafe once the struct gained non-trivial members); `CreateSurface`/`Lock`/`Unlock` call sites now go through `static_cast<IDirectDraw4*>`/`static_cast<IDirectDrawSurface4*>`. `meson test -C build`: 34/34 (2 pre-existing unrelated failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set (this file was already failing there for unrelated pre-existing reasons). Committed separately (`ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` Phase 6 commit).

    **`graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp` migrated off its own separate shadow type (2026-08-14)**: this file had its *own*, different local shadow interface, `DirectDrawSurfaceView` — an ABI-slot-accurate pure-virtual class (real COM slots: `blt` at 5, `restore` at 27) — removed in favor of the same canonical `platform/ddraw_interfaces.h::IDirectDrawSurface4`. Converted `DDRAW_PresentRect`'s 4 `->blt()`/2 `->restore()` raw-named-but-ABI-slotted calls and `UIPANEL_DestroySurface`'s `->release()` call to `->Blt()`/`->Restore()`/`->Release()`. `meson test -C build`: 34/34. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set.

    **New, more serious blocker found while starting `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`'s migration (2026-08-14): `UIPANEL_BeginPaint` (0x426B00) will `ExitProcess(1)` the whole game once `g_primary_surface` is wired.** `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`'s raw dispatch (`(*(void***)g_primary_surface)[0x44/4]`, real slot for `GetDC`) retries up to 1000 times at 10ms apiece, then calls `WIN32_FatalError()`/`ExitProcess(1)` on failure. `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::GetDC` is a permanent, deliberately-scoped no-op that always returns failure (`platform/ddraw_interfaces.h`'s Phase 3 note: "GetDC/ReleaseDC... real GDI/palette shim is separate, larger, tracked work"). So this is not a dormant-until-wired null-pointer skip like every other raw-dispatch site converted so far — it's a **timed self-destruct**: the moment `g_primary_surface` is non-null and any real caller of `UIPANEL_BeginPaint` (e.g. `game/BuildingPanel.cpp`, which has live, real call sites) actually runs, the process spins for ~10 seconds and then kills itself. **A real `GetDC` implementation (or a different, non-fatal path for these specific callers) is now a hard prerequisite for wiring `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer`, on top of the pixel-format fix above** — this is a bigger, separately-scoped piece of work (real GDI device-context interop), not a quick follow-up. Deliberately did NOT convert `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`'s raw dispatch to a named `GetDC()` call yet: doing so would still hit the exact same retry-loop/`ExitProcess` fate once wired, and leaving the raw-slot anti-pattern in place is the more honest reflection of "this call site is blocked," not "this call site is fixed." `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`'s edits (an include addition and a real, independent bug fix — the file's local `#define DDBLT_WAIT 0x00000010` is wrong; the real DirectX value from `platform/ddraw_interfaces.h` is `0x01000000`, `0x10` is actually `DDBLT_ASYNC`; harmless today since `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::Blt` ignores its `flags` parameter entirely) were reverted rather than left half-migrated, since no dispatch site had actually been converted. `UIPANEL_EndPaintEx`'s `Unlock` raw dispatch (slot 0x68/4) and `UIPANEL_Render`'s 7 `Blt` raw dispatch sites are clean, evidenced, low-risk conversions (signatures match exactly) — but they live in the same two functions as the blocked `GetDC`/paired-`Unlock` pair, so converting them should happen together with resolving `GetDC`, not before, to avoid a file left in a partially-migrated state again. `world/tilemap.cpp` also has raw vtable dispatch on `g_primary_surface`, not yet examined.

    **`GetDC`/`ReleaseDC` prerequisite RESOLVED (2026-08-14)**: `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::GetDC` now hands back a valid non-null opaque handle (the surface pointer itself) and returns success, instead of a permanent failure — the same "valid placeholder handle" idiom `graphics/sdl3_window.cpp` already uses for `CreateSolidBrush`/`LoadCursorA`/`LoadIconA`, safe because that file's HDC-consuming shims (`FillRect`/`DrawTextA`/`SetTextColor`/`SetBkMode`) already ignore their `hdc` argument entirely. Converted `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`/`UIPANEL_EndPaintEx`'s raw dispatch to named `GetDC`/`ReleaseDC` calls, fixing a real transcription bug a fresh Ghidra decompile of 0x426B00 exposed: the original checks `GetDC`'s HRESULT return (0 = success) and reads the actual HDC from the out-param at +0x4C, but the prior C++ treated the HRESULT itself as the HDC (`while (hdc == NULL)`) — misreading a successful 0/S_OK as failure, which would have spun to the 1000-retry `ExitProcess(1)` path even against a working `GetDC`. Also corrected `UIPANEL_EndPaintEx`'s slot-0x68 dispatch, previously mislabeled "Unlock": decompile of 0x426B90 confirms the value reaching that slot is a raw HDC (from `BeginPaint`), not a `RECT*` — i.e. `ReleaseDC(HDC)`, not `Unlock(RECT*)` — independently corroborated by `input/Cursor_internal.h`'s own prior finding on the same slot (sourced from `Cursor::render`, 0x414C28). Converted `UIPANEL_Render`'s remaining 7 raw `Blt` dispatch sites now that the file's other two blockers are gone. `meson test -C build`: 34/36 (2 pre-existing sandbox-only netns failures, unrelated). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 681-error set.

    **`world/tilemap.cpp`'s raw Lock/Unlock dispatch on `g_primary_surface` converted (2026-08-14)**: `TileMap_LockPrimarySurface`/`TileMap_UnlockPrimarySurface` dispatched through real ABI slots 25/32 directly — the same wrong-slot risk Phase 5(a) already fixed for `g_ddraw`'s sites, since this shim's own compiler-generated vtable order doesn't match those slot numbers. Neither call site reads the resulting `DDSURFACEDESC`'s contents (only the return code is checked), so both now construct a fresh local `DDSURFACEDESC` rather than reusing the class's own `ddsurfacedesc_buf` (+0x52494, `world/tilemap.h:400`) — that field is sized 0x7C bytes (the original x86 `sizeof(DDSURFACEDESC)`), too small for the real host struct (larger due to a real 8-byte `lpSurface` pointer and non-trivial `DDPIXELFORMAT`/`DDSCAPS2` members); reusing it would have been a real out-of-bounds write. Left `ddsurfacedesc_buf` itself alone (still zeroed by the constructor, now otherwise unused) rather than retyping/removing it — `TileMap` is a large class and retyping one field risks shifting every other raw-offset field access elsewhere in the file; that's a separate, dedicated layout audit. `meson test -C build`: 34/36 (same 2 pre-existing failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: identical error set (line-shifted only).

    **Cursor subsystem's raw dispatch RESOLVED (2026-08-14)** — the last blocker before `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer` can be wired. `input/Cursor_internal.h`'s 5 helper functions (`Cursor_ComSurfaceRelease`/`Cursor_SurfaceBlt`/`Cursor_SurfaceReleaseDC`/`Cursor_SurfaceFill`/`Cursor_SurfaceLegacyBlt`, real methods behind them: `Release` slot 2, `Blt` slot 5 — three differently-named helpers all pointed at the same slot, `ReleaseDC` slot 0x68/4=26) and all 28 call sites across `input/Cursor.cpp`/`Cursor_Editor.cpp`/`Cursor_Render.cpp`/`Cursor_impls.cpp`/`Cursor_new_impls.cpp` converted to named calls through `static_cast<IDirectDrawSurface4*>`; the 5 helpers and their typedefs removed from `Cursor_internal.h` (their own header comment claiming a permitted "opaque COM surface" ABI exception was stale — that applies only when no typed interface exists, which stopped being true once `platform/ddraw_interfaces.h` was added). Two real, independent bugs found and fixed while converting: `Cursor::draw_color_palette`'s `target_surf`/`drawTarget`/`cleanSurf` were typed `int*` (a "known object stored as an unrelated pointer type" landmine) — retyped to `void*`; `Cursor::draw_locomotive_preview`'s color-fill `Blt` reinterpret-cast a fabricated 2-int buffer (`{100, colorKey}`) as a `DDBLTFX*`, but this shim's `Blt()` genuinely reads `fx->dwFillColor` from that pointer for color-fills (`graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp`) — `dwFillColor` sits far past byte 8 in the real struct, so the old buffer was a real out-of-bounds read waiting to happen once `target_surf` became non-null; replaced with a real `DDBLTFX` with `dwFillColor` set by name. Also flagged, not fixed (out of scope): `draw_locomotive_preview`'s wipe-animation blit rects hold `{x, y, width, height}`-shaped values, not DirectDraw's real `{left, top, right, bottom}` `RECT` semantics — same class of pre-existing issue as the already-documented `DDBLT_WAIT`/`DDBLT_ASYNC` value swap. `meson test -C build`: 34/36 (2 pre-existing sandbox-only netns failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: identical 681-error set (line-shifted only, verified via `git stash` bisection at each of the 3 build checkpoints during the sweep).
    4. **Four live raw-`vtbl[N]` call sites on `g_ddraw` itself must convert to named calls *before* wiring, not after**: `native/ddraw_surface_ops.c` (`DDRAW_ReleaseSurfaces`, slot 2/20), `input/Cursor.cpp:543-548`, `input/Cursor_Editor.cpp:218-250` (both call `CreateSurface` via slot 6). Checked slot 6 against `ddraw_interfaces.h`'s actual (non-ABI, declaration-order) vtable: it does **not** land on `CreateSurface`. A non-null `g_ddraw` pointing at a real `Sdl3DirectDraw4` would make these four sites dispatch through the wrong compiler-generated slot — worse than today's null-pointer skip. Phase 6's raw-dispatch migration for these four `g_ddraw` sites is therefore a hard prerequisite for Phase 5(b), not a follow-up.
    Phase 5(a) (done, 2026-08-14): converted all four `g_ddraw` raw-dispatch sites to named calls (`native/ddraw_surface_ops.c`, `input/Cursor.cpp`, `input/Cursor_Editor.cpp`), each cast at the call boundary; `g_ddraw` stays `void*` at every *declaration* site (avoids forcing unrelated raw-dispatch rewrites — e.g. `input/Cursor.cpp`/`input/Cursor_Editor.cpp`'s `desc - 0x8C` stack-pointer arithmetic, a separate pre-existing landmine deliberately not touched; each `CreateSurface` call now builds a real, correctly-sized `DDSURFACEDESC` alongside the untouched legacy `int desc[24]`, translating its already-recovered field values 1:1 rather than reusing the undersized buffer as the real struct). Also converted `native/DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight.c`'s raw `vtbl[22]` `GetSurfaceDesc` dispatch (found live via `Cursor::init_sprites`'s backbuffer path during safety review) to a named call — same wrong-slot risk. `meson test -C build`: 33/33. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set.
    Phase 5(b) (done, 2026-08-14): wired `g_ddraw` to a real device. Considered and rejected hooking `core/CGWND.cpp`'s `InitAllSubsystems` (the plan's original suggestion) — direct investigation already found the *original* bring-up call chain (`ResourceManager::Init()` → `DDRAW_GetSurface`) is itself dead on the host build today (zero callers reach `ResourceManager::Init()`), so inserting a bring-up call there would be fabricating a call site with no evidence in the original control flow, not reproducing it. Instead used the sanctioned host-adapter pattern already proven elsewhere in this file: `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp`'s `SDL3_EnsurePrimarySurface()` (already called for real during menu rendering, `ui/GameSetupPanel.cpp`) now also assigns `g_ddraw = g_sdl_ddraw;` right after constructing the device, inside its existing `#ifndef _WIN32` block — one line, no new call site, no fabricated original behavior. `g_ddraw`'s defining declaration moved from `shared/stubs_impl.cpp` to a new dependency-free `platform/ddraw_globals.cpp` so small standalone unit tests linking `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp` can still link (added `obj_ddraw_globals` alongside every `obj_sdl3_ddraw` use in `tests/meson.build`). Verified safe before wiring: `GameWindow::create()`, `Cursor::show()`, and `Cursor::init_sprites()` (the three call sites that would exercise a non-null `g_ddraw` in a currently-unverified way) all have zero real callers anywhere in the tree today — confirmed dead, not just guarded. `DDRAW_ReleaseSurfaces` stays inert regardless (early-returns on `DAT_004A9908 == NULL`, itself never assigned). `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set.
    Phase 5(c) (done, 2026-08-14): wired `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer` to the real `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface` objects `SDL3_EnsurePrimarySurface()` already constructs internally (`g_sdl_primary_surface`/`g_sdl_backbuffer`, used for rendering since Phase 3) — a 2-line addition right after their construction, mirroring `g_ddraw`'s Phase 5(b) wiring exactly. Every prerequisite was already resolved earlier this session (pixel-format adapter, `GetDC`/`ReleaseDC`, and every raw-dispatch site on these two globals tree-wide). Consolidated both globals' defining declarations into `platform/ddraw_globals.cpp` alongside `g_ddraw`'s, out of `shared/link_stubs.cpp`/`shared/stubs_impl.cpp` respectively, for the same dependency-free-TU reason. `_g_primary_surface`/`_g_backbuffer` (finding 2, the underscore-prefixed pair sharing the same confirmed addresses) deliberately left unwired — that "very likely the same original global" finding is strong but not fully confirmed, so their few remaining Cursor-subsystem consumers stay dormant behind existing null checks rather than aliasing them without full certainty. This is the first time `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer` are non-null on host — `meson test -C build`: 34/36 (2 pre-existing sandbox-only netns failures) including `--suite integration`, all green, confirming the wiring introduces no crash/hang regression. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 681-error set. `nm -u`: `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer`/`g_ddraw` all resolve as expected, binary links clean.

    **Verified, not just inferred, whether the newly-live paint path actually fires (2026-08-14)**: added temporary `fprintf(stderr, ...)` probes to `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::Lock` and `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`, reran `--suite integration`, and grepped each test's `stderr.log` under `build/test-artifacts/`. Zero hits in any of the three mode-3 tests (`test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`, `test_singleplayer_mode3_mouse_input_reaches_game`, `test_singleplayer_mode3_click_reaches_wndproc_stream_stub`) — their `stderr.log`s have real content (212 lines each, including `DDRAW_Init` trace lines) but never call `Lock`/`BeginPaint`. So the earlier claim that these tests were "the first real end-to-end exercise" of `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`/`EndPaintEx`/`Render`/`TileMap_Lock/UnlockPrimarySurface` was **wrong** — a test passing before and after a behavior change doesn't demonstrate the change took effect, and these three are negative/reachability assertions that also pass when the path is simply never reached. The corrected claim: wiring `g_primary_surface`/`g_backbuffer` is verified **safe** (no crash, no hang, no `ExitProcess` regression) but **not yet verified exercised** — `BuildingPanel`'s paint entry points, the actual callers of `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`, need gameplay progression (opening a real building panel) beyond what the current integration suite drives. Probes reverted immediately after the check (`git diff --stat` confirmed clean).

    **Phase 5 is now complete** (wiring is done, correct, and safe). Remaining DirectDraw-shim work: (1) the separately-tracked `_g_primary_surface`/`_g_backbuffer` consolidation question (finding 2) — not a blocker, all of that pair's consumers are already null-guarded; (2) confirming the paint/lock path fires under real gameplay (opening a building panel) rather than just being reachable — not a blocker for the shim itself, but the honest current boundary of what's been demonstrated.
    **Dead-code claim strengthened via Ghidra `get_xrefs_to` (2026-08-14), not just C++ grep**: `Cursor::init_sprites` (0x414130) has exactly one real x86 caller, 0x413DE0 — the address `ui/GameWindow.cpp:532` documents as `GameWindow::create`. But `get_xrefs_to 0x413DE0` shows **4 real callers**: `CGWND_AboutDialog_Create` (0x40F5A1), `TrainStationWindow_Create` (0x436D49), `HelpWnd_Create` (0x450D40), plus 4 vtable DATA references — meaning 0x413DE0 is very likely a *shared* window-creation helper reused across at least 4 window classes (GameWindow/AboutDialog/TrainStationWindow/HelpWnd), not specifically `GameWindow::create` as currently labeled — worth the RE agent's attention, not resolved here (out of this shim task's scope). Reachability conclusion is unaffected either way: all 3 of `AboutDialog::Create`/`TrainStationWindow::Create`/`HelpWnd::create`'s own call sites in `core/CGWND.cpp::InitAllSubsystems` are `#ifdef _WIN32`-gated (lines ~1429/1458/1465, the `#else` branch) — dead on the actual host build regardless of which class 0x413DE0 really belongs to. `Cursor::init_sprites` (and therefore the `CreateSurface`/`DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight` calls fixed in it this session) stays confirmed dead on host today.
- [x] **`GameView::center_on_point` implemented for real (0x42D440, vtable[3], 2026-08-13)** — the last live-reachable stub from the Town→GameView restructuring. Full 186-instruction body reconstructed: client rect from four globals (`g_client_width/height/offset_x/offset_y`, offset by `g_viewport_x/y` via `OffsetRect`), deadzone from `this->resource->frame_width + (game_object_sub.resource->frame_width >> 1)`, flips `Panel::dim_flag` and repositions/re-renders every child `TrackPiece` when the clamped `center_x` crosses either deadzone edge, notifies via `StopSound(dim_flag)` (vtable slot 7 — a generic state-change callback here, not audio) on both `this` and `game_object_sub`, then repositions both via `MoveTo`. **Same vtable-recursion trap class as the `render_selection` fix above, caught by the subagent itself this time**: `GameView`'s own vtable overrides `Entity::MoveTo`'s slot ([3]) with `center_on_point` itself, so a virtual `this->MoveTo(...)` from inside it would recurse infinitely — the original's two repositions of `this` are confirmed-hardcoded `CALL 0x00405C00` (`Entity::MoveTo`), reconstructed as explicit `this->Entity::MoveTo(...)`; the paired `game_object_sub.MoveTo(...)` reposition stays an ordinary virtual call (unambiguous, `game_object_sub` is exactly an `Entity`). No new fields needed — the two `RESDATA` fields the child-reposition loops read were already canonical in `shared/types.h` under different names (`frame_w`/`world_x`). Found and routed around a second, still-open, out-of-scope linkage bug while wiring `OffsetRect`: `game/Panel.h`'s own `OffsetRect` declaration has no `extern "C"` and a different parameter type than the real definition (`graphics/sdl3_window.cpp`) — currently dead everywhere it's declared that way (matches the already-known `game/TrackPiece.cpp` workaround for the same symbol; not fixed, `Panel.h` is out of scope). `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: zero new errors (byte-identical to the established baseline for this file).
- [ ] **`GameView::postcard_command_handler`-equivalent slot (0x42D6B0, GameView vtable[17]) and `handle_tile_click` (0x42CE10) are very likely GameView's own methods, still attributed to `Town`** — same "ECX = bare immediate 0x4852A0" receiver evidence as the six methods moved off `Town` this session (see Completed), deliberately deferred. `Town.h`'s field model for +0x88..+0x1B8 was built from exactly these two methods and has not been re-verified against `GameView`'s real layout; do not trust it without re-deriving from these two methods' own disassembly first.
