Everywhere else in `GameView` (`select_building`, `deselect_building`, `update_selection`, and this same function's `0x3807`/`0x3808` branches) the `+0xE0` slot is a real `Building*` (matches `DDRAW_Building::SelectBuilding(Building*)`'s signature). But the `0x3806` branch's own disassembly (0x42D839/0x42D83F) reads `+0x44C` off whatever is stored there, which is `VehicleEditor::target_building` (`core/VehicleEditor.h:118`), not inside `Building`'s real 0xF4-byte layout at all — i.e. this branch's original author expected a `VehicleEditor*` in the same slot other branches treat as `Building*`. Converted the previous raw offset-cast read (`*reinterpret_cast<void**>(reinterpret_cast<intptr_t>(this->selected_building) + 0x44C)`, a real out-of-bounds read on a `Building*`) into a loud, documented stub rather than guess at a union/second field — the branch is also provably unreachable on host today regardless (see next bullet), so nothing regresses by stubbing it. Resolve by re-deriving whether `GameView`'s +0xE0 slot is mode-tagged (Building* during placement, VehicleEditor* during track editing) before re-enabling.
- [ ] **`Town::handle_tile_click` (0x42CE10) has zero real callers anywhere in the tree, and even a direct call would immediately hit a loud stub**: its child-creation call, `RESDATA_CreateChildSprite` (extern "C" shape, 0x4546D0, `shared/stubs_link001_batch3_resource_audio.cpp`), `assert(0)`s before returning any child. This means no `TrackPiece` with `resource_id` 0x3806/0x3807/0x3808 can exist in the current build, so `GameView::update_cursor_child`'s three resource-ID branches (reached only from `track_building`'s child loop) are dead code today — noted for whoever eventually wires up `handle_tile_click` for real, since that will make all three branches live simultaneously, including the unresolved `selected_building` type-identity bullet above.
- [ ] **Standing rule, not just a worklist for these 4 sites**: before placement-`new`-ing (or member-init-list-constructing into) a real C++ object whose allocation size came from x86 evidence (a literal like `0x2C`/`0x88`, or an original struct's documented x86 `sizeof`), always allocate via a `*_Size()` helper (or plain `sizeof(Type)` if the type is visible in that TU) instead of the literal — never assume the x86 size still matches once the type has any pointer-typed field, since those widen on this 64-bit host. This bit twice this session: `ScriptedObject::sub_entity[0x88]` (caught, worked around, not fixed) and, live, `resources/AssetMgr.cpp`'s `AssetMgr::AssetMgr` construction in `TileMap::TileMap()` — the latter shipped with the undersized `operator_new(0x2C)` literal for one full edit-build-test cycle before the integration suite's first GUI test caught a real glibc heap-corruption crash (`AssetMgr_Size()` fixed it; see the `retire-spritedata` milestone). The type-identity question ("is this really an X") and the size question ("is the allocation big enough for X on this host") are separate checks — resolving the first does not resolve the second, and a green `meson test` does not confirm the second either, since heap corruption doesn't always crash on the exact call that caused it. **Size-audit the 4 remaining `GameObject_BaseCtor`/`Entity::Entity` (0x405790) call sites before wiring any of them for real** (found 2026-08-14, see Completed milestone above for the two already-fixed size-safe sites). Each embeds or references storage that may be smaller than host `sizeof(Entity)` (168 bytes) — real construction there would silently overflow into adjacent fields, the same class of bug already documented and deliberately worked around (not fixed) for `ScriptedObject::sub_entity[0x88]`. Gating prerequisite for each, in ascending order of expected difficulty:
  - `world/scriptengine.cpp:582` (`this->gameobject`, declared `uint8_t gameobject[0x87]`, +0xE0) — 135 bytes, 33 short. Same landmine as `ScriptedObject::sub_entity`, not yet documented as unsafe anywhere before this pass; the whole class already reads through it via `reinterpret_cast<Entity*>`/`reinterpret_cast<GameObject*>` and manual `entity_view`/`panel_view` helper casts (`game/Panel.h`'s cast-based "view" anti-pattern, not yet fixed) — needs the same care as `ScriptedObject`'s resolution before any change, including the manual-cast call sites, not just the ctor.
  - `graphics/DDRAW_Building.h:83` (`sub_object_1`, declared `GameObject sub_object_1;`, +0xE0) — typed as the *base* class (`sizeof(GameObject)` per its own header is 0x24/36 bytes), not `Entity` — far too small for a real `Entity` in place. Needs retyping the field itself to `Entity`, which shifts every subsequent field's offset in `DDRAW_Building`'s ~0x4B0-byte layout — a full re-layout, not a one-line fix.
  - `graphics/DDRAW_Building.h:106,122,129` (`popup_panel`/`pattern_container`/`track_sprite`, all declared `void*`) — `GameObject_BaseCtor(&popup_panel, ...)` etc. take the address of the *pointer field itself* (8 bytes on this host), not of allocated storage; the header's own comment calls these "Embedded GameObject", i.e. the original x86 layout embeds the object inline at that offset and the host reconstruction mistakenly modeled the field as a bare pointer. Needs the same retype-to-`Entity`-member treatment as `sub_object_1`, times three, each shifting the same shared struct's later offsets again.
  - Do not dispatch a single mechanical sweep across all 4 — `DDRAW_Building.h`'s three fixes compound on the same struct's offsets and must be done together or not at all; `scriptengine.cpp`'s fix is independent but touches many more call sites (the `entity_view`/`panel_view`/`reinterpret_cast` pattern) than just the constructor. Each is its own dedicated pass.

### Priority 2 — missing/deferred original logic

- [ ] **`PoolAllocator` is almost certainly a misattributed duplicate of `ResourceManager` itself, not a separate class — but a genuine vtable/layout conflict blocks merging it (corrected 2026-08-15, supersedes this item's original 2026-08-15 framing, which was wrong)**: the original framing of this item claimed "`PoolAllocator`'s own field layout is a genuine pool-allocator array, nothing like `ResourceManager`'s real layout" — that claim was made without reading `ResourceManager.h`'s own documented internal layout and is **false**. Resolving both of `PoolAllocator::Shutdown()`'s (0x445FE0) real callers via `decode_instructions` (not `disassemble_function`, since neither caller was bound into a Ghidra function) found **both pass `ECX = 0x4855e8 = &g_resmgr` directly**: the tail-call thunk at 0x45C580 (`MOV ECX, 0x4855e8; JMP 0x00445fe0` — this is the call `get_xrefs_to` reports as originating from "0x45C585", the `JMP` instruction's own address) and a sibling thunk at 0x45C560 that calls `NET_Lock` (0x445F70) the same way. `PoolAllocator`'s documented layout (`network/NetHelpers.h`) — `+0x2C: freelist[0x4000]` (0x10000 bytes, ending +0x1002B), `+0x10030: alloc_flags[0x4001]` (0x10004 bytes, ending +0x20033), total size ~0x20034 — lines up almost exactly with `ResourceManager.h`'s own documented `+0x002C: resource_type_idx (int32*[0x4001])` and `+0x10030: resource_ptrs (void*[0x4001])`, and `PoolAllocator`'s ~0x20034-byte size is a near-exact *prefix* of `ResourceManager`'s real documented 0x241BC-byte size. Two independent lines of evidence (receiver identity + field-layout correspondence) agree: `NET_Lock`/`NET_Unlock`/`PoolAllocator::Shutdown` (0x445F70/0x445FC0/0x445FE0) are very likely real, currently-unmerged methods of `ResourceManager` itself. **Gating conflict, not yet resolved — do not merge without settling this first**: `ResourceManager.h`'s own header explicitly claims "non-virtual class (no vtable)", but `NET_Lock`/`NET_Shutdown` both write a literal (`0x478270`) into `[this+0]`, and `read_bytes`/`get_xrefs_to` on `0x478270` confirm it is a **real 4-entry function-pointer table** (slot[0] = `NET_Unlock`/0x445FC0 itself, confirmed via `get_xrefs_to(0x445FC0)` showing its only xref is a DATA reference from 0x478270; slots [1..3] = 0x447B60/0x4489D0/0x448C90, not yet identified) — genuine vtable-shaped data, contradicting "no vtable." Also: `network/NetHelpers.h`'s own claim that `PoolAllocator` uses "dispatch tables at 0x47826C during initialization" is itself wrong — `get_xrefs_to(0x47826C)` shows its only real referencers are `NetworkPlayerList_ctor` (0x443013) and `NetworkPlayerList_Term` (0x4431FB), i.e. `0x47826C` is `NetworkPlayerList`'s own real vtable (already documented in `NetworkPlayerList.h`: slot[0] = its scalar deleting destructor, matching `0x4431F0`'s presence there), completely unrelated to `PoolAllocator`. Next concrete steps: (1) identify what real class the 4-entry table at `0x478270` belongs to and what its slots [1..3] (0x447B60/0x4489D0/0x448C90) really are — this determines whether `ResourceManager` genuinely needs virtual dispatch after all, or whether this table is some other manually-managed mode/state pointer unrelated to C++ vtables; (2) once resolved, fold `Lock()`/`Shutdown()` (0x445FE0, needs a name distinct from the already-real `ResourceManager::Shutdown()` at 0x446340) into `ResourceManager`'s one canonical header along with the presently-undocumented `+0x04..+0x27` fields (`counter1..4`, a `+0x18` file-data sub-object matching `DDRAW_FileData_Dtor`), and re-audit every real caller of `NET_Lock`/`NET_Unlock`/`PoolAllocator` tree-wide; (3) resolve `NET_Unlock`'s own identity separately — its only xref is the DATA reference from the `0x478270` table itself, not a direct call site, so its real caller chain (whatever dereferences that table's slot[0]) is still unconfirmed. This is a dedicated pass with real regression surface (`ResourceManager` is a ~30-file-included value-typed global; adding fields or a vtable changes `sizeof`/layout for every translation unit that embeds or references `g_resmgr`), not a quick merge — deliberately not attempted this tick per advisor review. **Note for anyone reading `git log`**: commit `9ca7dab`'s message description of `PoolAllocator` as "a genuine large pool-allocator array, not `ResourceManager`'s real layout" is superseded by this finding and should not be trusted.

- [ ] **`RESMGR_LoadSoundResource` (0x448D60) still has no real implementation — the only remaining half of this item, narrowed 2026-08-15 (`ReleaseSoundResource(ResourceEntry*)` is now complete, see Completed milestones)**: fully decompiled and disassembled. Body: increments `entry->refcount`; if `entry->buffer` already set, returns success immediately; otherwise zero-inits a 0x20-byte `WaveLoadBuf` (layout already documented in `native/wave_io.c`'s header comment: +0x00 unknown, +0x04 a `WAVEFORMATEX`-shaped format block, +0x18 `data_size`, +0x1C `data_buffer`), calls `Game_LoadWaveFile(entry->path, &waveBuf)`, builds a `DSBUFFERDESC` (`dwSize=0x14`, `dwFlags=0xCA`, or `0x80CA` if `entry->mode_flag != 0`, `dwBufferBytes=waveBuf.data_size`, `lpwfxFormat=&waveBuf+4`), rejects `wBitsPerSample < 8` with `E_NOTIMPL` (`0x80004001`), otherwise calls `g_audio->Play(&desc, &entry->buffer, nullptr)` (already-implemented, confirmed matching call shape), then `Lock()`s the created buffer (`offset=0, bytes=0, flags=DSBLOCK_ENTIREBUFFER=2`), memcpy's the loaded WAV data into the locked region, `Unlock()`s, frees the temp WAV buffer via `CRT_free`, and sets `entry->is_valid=1`. **The one unresolved blocker**: manually tracing the `Lock()` call's stack argument setup to identify which two stack addresses receive its `region1`(`void**`)/`bytes1`(`uint32_t*`) out-params produced two candidate addresses 0xC apart that couldn't be reconciled with the post-call reads that consume them for the `memcpy` — this is a memory-safety-relevant detail (wrong region1/bytes1 assignment could mean copying to the wrong destination or with the wrong length) that must be resolved with more precision than manual byte-offset arithmetic before writing this function; a Ghidra-assisted stack-frame/structure re-analysis of `0x448D60`'s Lock call site specifically (not a full re-decompile) is the next step. **The tree-wide sprawl is much larger than a quick "retype and unify" pass (checked 2026-08-15, corrects an earlier under-estimate)**: a full grep found at least 10 files with their own independent declarations of `RESMGR_LoadSoundResource`/`RESMGR_ReleaseSoundResource`, not the ~5 first assumed: production call sites in `ui/TrainStationWindow.cpp` (`int strRes`, live), `ui/ButtonSprite.cpp` (`void* res`), `ui/NameEntryPanel.cpp` (`int handle`, live), `core/GameObject.cpp` (`void* resource`, live, and its own address comment is a literal placeholder `0x447xxx`), `native/NETMAN_NetworkUI.c` (a `.c` file — C linkage, binds by bare name regardless of declared type), `core/Game.cpp` (`void* res`, 4 live call-site pairs), and `network/Netman.h` (declares a *third* name entirely, `ResourceManager_LoadSoundResource`, commented "was RESMGR_LoadSoundResource"); plus two test-local link fixtures (`tests/entity_host_resource_test.cpp`, `tests/persistence_fixtures.h`'s `LOUD_FIXTURE(RESMGR_ReleaseSoundResource)`) that provide their own no-op stand-ins matching `core/GameObject.cpp`'s exact signature to satisfy the linker for isolated test binaries — retyping the production declaration means updating these fixtures too, or the affected test binaries stop linking. **Bigger blocker found while checking whether `AudioChannel::LoadSound`'s call could simply be retyped to the canonical signature**: `AudioChannel::LoadSound` (`audio/AudioChannel.cpp:208`) receives a `SoundResource*`, not a `ResourceEntry*` — and `SoundResource` (`audio/AudioChannel.h`, a separate "ResourceEntry sound view" class) is not proven to be layout-identical to `ResourceEntry` beyond the fields both happen to document early (`buffer` at +0x0C). Unifying the two objects (or proving they're genuinely distinct) is itself a real object-model question, on top of the `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile`/`ResourceEntry`-class-hood prerequisites already tracked above — this whole area (resource loading + sound-resource identity) needs one dedicated, ground-up object-model recovery pass, not a sequence of small unrelated patches. **False lead ruled out while checking this**: `core/GameObject.cpp:218`'s `reinterpret_cast<void*>(static_cast<uintptr_t>(this->sound_res_id))` looked like a sign-extension bug at first glance, but `sound_res_id` is declared `uint32_t` (`core/Entity.h:58`), not `int32_t` — the cast zero-extends correctly, matching `ResourceManager.cpp`'s own safe `pointer_from_handle` pattern. Not a bug; don't re-flag it.
- [ ] **`RESMGR_AllocResourceEntry` is a `void`-returning stub used as if it returns `ResourceEntry*` — full scope now identified, three-function dependency chain, not a quick fix (found 2026-08-15, predates this session's work)**: real address `0x448990`, `__thiscall`, exactly one real caller (`ResourceManager_LoadStringTable` @ `0x446DF6`, confirmed via `get_xrefs_to`). Decompiled body: writes the vtable pointer `0x478278` directly into `this` (a real in-place-constructor pattern — matches CLAUDE.md's "constructor vtable writes → remove, C++ emits them," but `ResourceEntry` is currently a plain `struct` with `void* vtable` as a literal data member, not yet the real polymorphic class its own header comment already documents — slot[0]=destructor, slot[1]=`OpenResourceFile`/Parse), sets `resource_id`, conditionally `sprintf`s the `.wav` path into `+0x18`, unconditionally calls `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile(this)`, and returns `this`. **The real blocker is the dependency chain, not the stub itself**: `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile` (`0x448A70`) — the function `AllocResourceEntry` unconditionally calls — has zero definitions anywhere in the tree, not even a stub; `nm` shows it neither defined nor undefined, meaning nothing currently references it at all. Decompiled it too: a substantial, SEH-protected, ~90-instruction function with three fallback resource-resolution paths (`AssetMgr_LoadFile` via install-path-stripped name, `WIN32_StreamOpenPath` direct file open, `GetFileAttributesA` existence check) and a `vtable[1]` virtual dispatch to parse the loaded data — comparable in size to this session's earlier "Stream tail"/`WIN32_StreamMem` multi-tick efforts, not a single-sitting fix. `resources/ResourceManager.cpp:155-171` already carries a prior session's note on how to build it for real (a genuine `WIN32_Stream` RAII local, with the correct real addresses for `WIN32_StreamOpen`/`OpenPath`/`Read`/`DestroyImmediate` already cross-referenced from other files) — read that before attempting it. **Ordered prerequisites for whoever picks this up**: (1) recover `ResourceEntry`'s real object model as a polymorphic class (compiler-managed vtable, real virtual `Parse`/destructor slots) before writing any constructor logic against it, per CLAUDE.md's "model before implementation"; (2) implement `RESMGR_OpenResourceFile` for real against that model; (3) implement `RESMGR_AllocResourceEntry` as a real constructor (or placement-new factory) using both, and fix `create_string_resource`'s call site to placement-new into the pre-allocated `operator_new(300)` block instead of calling a free function. Do not attempt any single piece of this without the others — a fixed `AllocResourceEntry` still calling an absent `OpenResourceFile` doesn't close the gap. The failure mode as it stands today is exactly as originally reported: whenever `create_string_resource` runs (reachable via `PlaySound`/`PlaySoundAt`/`ResourceManager::AnimateClock`'s already-established real callers, predating this session), `entry` is garbage and `entry->is_valid` is a wild read.

## Architecture notes

### Class hierarchy

```
GameObject (0x477820, type=1)
  └── Entity (0x477488, type=2)
        ├── Building (0x477EB8, type=7, size 0xF4)
        │     └── Train (size 0xF0)
        ├── ResourceGameObject (was BuildingMgrObjectGroup)
        ├── Vehicle
        └── ... (Town, ScriptedObject, etc.)
BuildingMgr (0x477F70, size 0x7C) — separate class, not in GameObject tree
CGWND — application window class
```

### Key addresses

| Symbol | Address | Notes |
|--------|---------|-------|
| `VTBL_GAMEOBJECT` | 0x477820 | 6-slot root vtable |
| `VTBL_ENTITY` | 0x477488 | 15-slot intermediate vtable |
| `VTBL_BUILDING` | 0x477EB8 | Full Building vtable |
| `VTBL_BUILDINGMGR` | 0x477F70 | BuildingMgr core vtable |
| `Building::BaseCtor` | 0x433A20 | Shared ctor (Building + Train) |
| `Building::Deserialize` | 0x435700 | Save file loader |
| `operator_new` | 0x465CE0 | Global heap allocator |
| `GLOBAL_free` | 0x465CD0 | Global heap deallocator |
| `g_game_time` | 0x4A99B4 | Game tick counter |
| `g_main_window` | 0x4AA4A0 | Main HWND |

### Dual-vtable construction pattern

The binary uses intermediate vtables during construction:
- Building_BaseCtor sets vtable 0x477F18, then Building_Ctor overrides to 0x477EB8
- In natural C++, the compiler handles vtable progression through the ctor chain
- We do not emulate this pattern — we let C++ virtual dispatch work naturally

### C/C++ linkage in the original binary

The original `loco.exe` (MSVC 1998, x86) used C linkage for:
- All Win32 API imports (kernel32.dll, user32.dll, gdi32.dll) — inherently C-linkage
- Internal helpers: `operator_new`, `GLOBAL_free`, CRT wrappers
- Free functions compiled as `__cdecl` or `__fastcall` (e.g., `ButtonSprite_Ctor`, `Sprite_Init`)

57 decompiled files preserve these `extern "C"` declarations correctly.
The hybrid #ifdef approach eliminates the resulting ABI mismatch for simple
APIs while keeping the shim for complex subsystems (DDRAW, DSOUND, DPLAY).

### Key files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/link_stubs.cpp` | C + C++ stub definitions (~400 symbols) |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/stubs_impl.cpp` | Runtime stubs + globals |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/shared/crt_stubs.cpp` | CRT compatibility implementations |
| `src/decompiled_cpp/stubs/compat.h` | MSVC calling convention macros, type stubs |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_window.cpp` | Win32 windowing → SDL3 (to be deprecated by #ifdef) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_ddraw.cpp` | IDirectDraw4 → SDL3 Renderer (keep) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_dsound.cpp` | IDirectSound → SDL3 Audio (keep) |
| `src/sdl3_shims/sdl3_dplay.h` | DirectPlay stub (keep) |
| `tools/re_daemon/frontend/src/graph/contracts.ts` | Renderer-neutral dashboard graph and layout contracts |
| `tools/re_daemon/frontend/src/graph/GraphCanvas.tsx` | Swappable graph-renderer registry (React Flow initially) |

Identified `ResourceGameObject` (`core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.h`, ctor 0x4580a0) as the real canonical class every `TileMap::FindObject`-placed object either is or chains through, added real named members at Ghidra-verified offsets (`group_active`/`occupancy_links`/`occupancy_scores`/`occupancy_more`/`build_links`/`build_scores`/`build_more`, +0xC0..+0x108, `static_assert`-guarded), deleted `TileMapObject` outright, and converted all ~54 call sites tree-wide (`world/tilemap.h`/`.cpp`, `input/InputMgr.cpp`, `game/Building.cpp`) off `reinterpret_cast<TileMapObject*>`/the local `as_tilemap_object`/`as_entity` helpers onto direct typed member access or `static_cast<ResourceGameObject*>`. Root-cause-fixed `ScrollRect`'s displaceable check (`is_moving`→`group_active`) and an independently-disassembly-verified `ScrollTo` `+0x06`→`+0x18` (`GameObject::initialized`) mistake. Left one evidence gap honestly undecided rather than guessed at (`Building::RemoveOccupant`'s `tile_entry`, reached via unrelated evidence, stays a documented raw offset read, not a `ResourceGameObject*` cast). The fix immediately exposed two more real, previously-dormant bugs on the exact same load path — both diagnosed via `coredumpctl` and fixed: `INPUT_RemoveObject` (0x41DEF0) had been mis-classified in an earlier session as "editor-only" and left as a `std::abort()` stub, but `TileMap::ScrollTo` reaches it for real from `INPUT_LoadSaveFile`; fully decompiled and implemented it (collection removal, scenery/track neighbour cascade, message box, real `delete`), documenting a re-entrant-cascade recursion hazard the translation introduces (guarded correctly by the existing `initialized` gate — verified the guard is sufficient, not just asserted) and a `classify()` caveat inherited from `ScrollRect`'s own precedent. The new function's scenery-classification helper then hit the identical `RESDATA_IsSceneryTile` pointer-truncation crash already fixed elsewhere for `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile`/`IsRoadTile` — fixed the same way (host tile-type-byte accessor instead of a truncating direct call). A third crash surfaced after both fixes, reproduced 3 times with a stable call stack (`classify_tile_track_type`'s `unordered_map<string,...>::find` inside `ResourceManagerSdl3::get_sprite_by_id`, reached from `TileMap::FindObject`'s very first statement) — diagnosed as genuinely outside this item's `TileMapObject`/`ResourceGameObject` scope (no touched field/member is on that call path) and tracked as its own new Priority-1 blocker rather than chased further or papered over. Coverage could not be re-measured as a result: **before 84.21%, after not measurable** (mode-3 load now crashes during the save-replay loop, later than before this fix, before the fix's own success can be observed on screen) — reported plainly per this task's explicit "an honestly-reported partial outcome is acceptable" instruction, not estimated. `meson compile -C build`: clean. `meson test -C build`: unaffected. `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 4 failures, all via the new third-layer crash (pre-existing 2-3 netns failures unchanged). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: no new failure category (the 5 new `static_assert(offsetof(...))` lines trigger the same pre-existing `-Werror=invalid-offsetof` diagnostic the header's own pre-existing asserts already triggered); a clean before/after diff wasn't obtainable in this tree state (`git stash`-ing just these files breaks the link against the ~40-file concurrent DirectDraw/SDL3 WIP already mid-flight in this tree, confirmed by attempting it). |

### Historical milestone digest — 2026-08-02 through 2026-08-11 (compacted 2026-08-13)

- **Aug 2–5 — foundation and build migration:** established the partial audit inventories, stabilized mode-3 bootstrap, reconstructed initial Win32/thread/stream/network and UI pieces, flattened the source tree, retired build exclusions, and migrated the complete build/test surface to Meson. Historical intermediate pass counts are superseded by current gates.
- **Aug 6 — linkage and first mode-3 stability:** fixed the mode-3 transition/render/input linkage chain, corrected the UIPANEL/Cursor/Town caller families, removed `--allow-multiple-definition`, and established symbol/disassembly-based LINK-001 verification. This brought the then-current GUI gate to 12/12.
- **Aug 8–9 — strict typed cleanup:** completed the large cast/signature/field-access cleanup across Vehicle, Building, World, Train, Netman, DirectPlay, Town, ScriptEngine, native asset/session files, and DirectX adapters. The durable result is the 2026-08-09 tree-wide `-Dstrict=2` clean milestone; detailed residual architecture/linkage items live under **Remaining work** and `docs/landmine-sweep-worklist.md`.
- **Aug 9–10 — object-model corrections:** wired real mode-3 SDL mouse dispatch from `MainWndProc` evidence; recovered ArrivalQueue/ScrollCollection; converted DirectPlay to a class; proved `ChildWindow` is a genuine base class of `CursorEditWindow`, `TrainStation`, and `BuildingDescriptorEditor`; implemented their typed render paths; and removed a falsely attributed `CursorEditWindow::cleanup` EH helper.
- **Aug 10–11 — stream and runtime hardening:** diagnosed and replaced the invalid MSVC-vbtable stream facade, implemented the reachable `WIN32_StreamOpen*` path, fixed `CursorEditWindow::Render`'s signature, and added a crash-sensitive mode-3 click regression. Completed the LINK-001 sweep (`call 0`: 232→0), audited hardcoded `operator_new` sizes, and resolved the remaining Ghidra `FUN_` functions. Unresolved allocation/model findings from that audit are retained in **Remaining work**.
- Superseded per-commit diagnostics, temporary worktree status, stale failure counts, reverted attempts, and already-closed TODO narratives were removed. Canonical source comments, current Remaining-work items, Completed milestones, and referenced design/worklist documents retain the durable evidence needed for future work.

**2026-08-12 (tile-placement-metadata-re-breakthrough)**: Closed the "genuinely open, unscoped RE question" from the "Town rendering — Phase B" item above (now updated in place, see "Remaining work"). Confirmed via `strings -a resource.RFD | grep -c physical_occupancy` (440 hits) that the tile-footprint metadata `TileMap` needs is real, uncompressed, plaintext inside the shipped `art-res/resource.RFD` archive, and that its parser (`BuildingDescriptorEditor`'s `physical_occupancy`/`bitmap_occupancy` directive cascade, 0x41E9F0, plus `ChildWindow::Render`'s base sprite/animation cascade, 0x424E00) is already fully reverse-engineered and integrated — it was never missing, just not yet connected to placement. Root cause of the earlier dead end: `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_AddChild` (0x44B190) constructs a `BuildingDescriptorEditor`-shaped object (calls its exact base constructor) with a distinct, still-unmodeled vtable (`0x478358`) rather than a real `ScriptedObject` — confirming a prior session's suspicion (line 252's 2026-08-11 note) instead of the "97 UI/dialog callers, wrong pathway" doubt right below it. Implemented a first verified slice: `resources/resource_manager_sdl3.{h,cpp}` gained a `SpriteFootprint` field on `SpriteMetadata`, parsed from real archive data and checked against resource `0x341d` (`roads\half-vwint.dat`) in `tests/resource_manager_sdl3_test.cpp`. Also fixed a real, separate `has_metadata` gating bug this surfaced (see item above). `meson test -C build`: 31/31 including integration. Placement wiring itself (un-gating `host_placement_available()`) is not done — see the three follow-up steps listed in the updated item.

**2026-08-12 (tile-placement-metadata-followup)**: Continuing the breakthrough above, found the real minimum-field placement consumer: `TileMap::FindObject` (0x4550C0), not `TileMap::ScrollTo` as first guessed (`ScrollTo` only fires on already-placed, in-motion objects — verified by re-reading its own early-return guard). `FindObject` needs `grid_width/height/depth/span_y/original_span` plus per-cell `occupancy_grid` data populated by `INPUT_PlaceObject` (0x41DD80, still a loud deferred stub) — a materially larger dependency chain than "just parse the bounding box," now documented in place of the earlier under-scoped note. Also found and decompiled a previously-undefined function (0x44B4F0, vtable `0x478358` slot `[3]`) that produces `TileMapResource::state_63A`'s track-type classification byte — real, evidenced (all keyword strings read directly), but its caller is unidentified (`get_xrefs_to` found no call site), so left as its own tracked item per the advisor's explicit steer not to fold two different fields/consumers into one story or chase a callerless 2000-byte function without a known reachability path. No code changes this entry — Ghidra comments + PROGRESS.md only; `resources/resource_manager_sdl3.*`/tests unchanged from the prior commit.

**2026-08-12 (input-placeobject-investigation)**: Set out to implement `INPUT_PlaceObject` per the prior entry's plan. Decompiled it (a clean resource-type dispatcher to four already-real constructors) and its collection-Insert dependency (0x412440, growth factor confirmed as exactly -1.1 via `read_bytes` on the FMUL constant at 0x477838) — both fully scoped and ready. Before writing code, measured against the real bootstrap instead of trusting this file's own prior claim that `host_placement_available()` gates placement: it's already `true` in production (`HostMode3Bootstrap.cpp:287`), yet grepping the real GUI-test stderr log shows `INPUT_PlaceObject` is never reached at all — `TileMap::ScrollRect` silently rejects every one of the 497 records first, because it reads `TileMapObject`-shaped offsets (`grid_width`/`occupancy_grid`) directly off the raw `SpriteResource*` the host resource manager returns, before any real object exists. Found the same unguarded-offset pattern already merged in `ResourceGameObject`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s constructors (INTEGRATED status). This is the actual blocker, documented as a new landmine-bug-class item above; `INPUT_PlaceObject`/`ListInsert` deliberately left unwritten (would be untestable dead code) — the evidence trail is preserved for whoever fixes the host resource-adapter gap. No source changes this session; Ghidra comments + PROGRESS.md only.

**2026-08-12 (host-resource-adapter-first-slice)**: Continued the `INPUT_PlaceObject`/landmine thread. Added the first piece of a real host resource adapter: `resources/resource_manager_sdl3.{h,cpp}` gained a magic-tagged `SpriteResource::magic` field (placed after every offset `EditWindow`'s ABI asserts on) and an exported `is_host_sprite_resource()` check; `world/tilemap.cpp`'s `TileMap::ScrollRect` now uses it (`#ifndef _WIN32`) to reject loudly instead of reading `grid_width`/`occupancy_grid` off a raw `SpriteResource*`. Measuring the real GUI-test run against this new guard (expecting ~497 hits, proving the landmine item above was the blocker) instead got **zero** hits — the tell that the real blocker was upstream. Extracted the actual `curr` save file a test run produced and read its header directly (`entity_count=497`, ruling out a save-path miscount), then traced the true cause: `core/HostMode3Bootstrap.cpp`'s `HostLoadingSequence` called `set_host_placement_available(true)` *after* `INPUT_LoadWorld("curr")` — the one call that walks all 497 records — instead of before, so every record silently took the "gate closed, carry it" branch during the load that mattered. Fixed (moved the call earlier); re-measured at **exactly 497** guard hits, 0 skips. `meson test -C build`: 31/31 including integration, unchanged. Corrected the prior entry's "actual blocker" framing (the raw-offset-read landmine is real and now confirmed *reached*, but the ordering bug was upstream of it) and the matching memory files. Real placement still requires the host resource adapter (occupancy-grid cells + the fields `ResourceGameObject`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle` read) before `INPUT_PlaceObject`/`ListInsert` can be written — not attempted this entry, per the advisor's explicit "don't start the occupancy adapter in the same commit" guidance.

**2026-08-12 (childwindow-descriptor-fields)**: Continued resolving `INPUT_PlaceObject`'s remaining raw-offset reads. Traced `core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`'s `ResourceGameObject::ResourceGameObject` (`resource+0x522`/`+0x14D`/`+0x20`) and `INPUT_PlaceObject`'s own tail (`+0x62c` off the constructed entity's `resource` field, `core/Entity.h:44`) against the already-INTEGRATED `ui/UI_ChildWindow.h`/`input/BuildingDescriptorEditor.h` class layouts instead of guessing: `+0x14D` = `ChildWindow::name` ("Name" directive), `+0x20` = `ChildWindow::heapBuffer` (a runtime frame-set entry-table pointer, not a parsed scalar), `+0x522` = `BuildingDescriptorEditor::ee_replay_delay` ("EEReplayDelay" directive, clamped ≤5 — **not** `MaxMinifigForResource`, an initial guess corrected by reading the actual field name), and `+0x62c` = `BuildingDescriptorEditor::leisure_destination` ("LeisureDestination" directive — the same directive that motivated the `physical_occupancy` row-counted parser two entries ago). Considered and rejected constructing real `BuildingDescriptorEditor` objects from `ResourceManager_GetById` (would put the `WNDPROC_Stream*`/`AssetMgr_LoadFile` stack on every live resource load and break every existing typed `GetById` caller) — stayed on the additive `SpriteMetadata` layer instead. Added `SpriteMetadata::name`/`ee_replay_delay`/`leisure_destination` (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.{h,cpp}`), verified against three real resources: `0x816` (`building\factory1.dat`, `"Name Factory"`), `0x852` (`building\launcher.dat`, the only shipped `"EEReplayDelay -1"` — confirms the `uint8_t` wrap-then-clamp produces 5, not -1 or 255), and `0x1020` (`scenery\bigfount.dat`, `"LeisureDestination 1"`). `meson test -C build`: 31/31 including integration. **Still open before writing `INPUT_PlaceObject`**: `heapBuffer`/`+0x20` is read as `frames[anim_index].wait_time` — need to confirm in the binary (not guess from the `.dat` text) which parsed animation-row column the original writes to `FrameData::wait_time` (+0x08), and confirm what `anim_index` actually holds for a `Entity(resource_id, -1, 0, 0)`-constructed object (a `-1` index would read `std::vector[-1]` on host, unlike the original's harmless-ish OOB heap read) before assuming `frame_sets[anim_index]` is safe to read directly. Also still open: `INPUT_PlaceObject`'s construction-failure check (`+0x18` on the constructed object, tested before insertion) and replacing its four x86-literal `operator_new` sizes with `sizeof(Class)`, per the `ResourceManager.cpp`/`BuildingDescriptorEditor` precedent.

**2026-08-12 (entity-initbase-host-safety)**: Before writing `INPUT_PlaceObject`, found the fix was scoped one layer too shallow: guarding `ResourceGameObject`'s own constructor body is moot because `Entity`'s BASE constructor runs first and calls `Entity::InitBase` (0x405900, `core/GameObject.cpp`), which was still fully unguarded — reading raw RESDATA offsets via `field_at<T>()` and, critically, calling through `ResourceDataView`'s virtual `acquire_surface`/`release_surface` slots (1/2). Checked `resource_manager_sdl3.cpp`'s `k_resource_vtable`: slots 1 and 2 are `nullptr`. So the failure mode here isn't silent OOB corruption like the other landmine instances — it's an immediate null-pointer virtual-call crash, and it happens *before* `ResourceGameObject`'s own body even executes. Confirmed nothing on the host currently constructs an `Entity`-derived object with a real `resource_id` (31/31 green precisely because this path is unreached, not because it's safe).

Added `#ifndef _WIN32` host branches to the three methods actually on the construction path — `Entity::InitBase`, `Entity::SetAnimState`, `Entity::SetFrame` — plus `Entity::~Entity`'s matching `release_surface` call, replacing every raw-offset read with a host source already resolved by prior entries in this chain: frame dims → `sprite_width()`/`sprite_height()` (already exported), "has surface" → `sprite_bitmap() != nullptr`, `default_anim` → `SpriteMetadata::cursor_frame`, `anim_count`/`anim_table` → `frame_sets.size()`/`frame_sets[i]`, resource-identity check → new `sprite_resource_id()` accessor (added alongside `sprite_width`/`sprite_height`). `blit_flags` (`+0x164`) has no host source and defaults to 0. `acquire_surface`/`release_surface` are skipped entirely for host sprites — they're owned by `ResourceManagerSdl3`'s cache, not per-entity refcounted.

Added a **reachability proof**, not just a guard: `tests/entity_host_resource_test.cpp` constructs a real `Entity(0x1020, -1, 0, 0)` against the REAL `resource_manager_sdl3.cpp` bridge (deliberately not `tests/persistence_fixtures.h`, which fakes `ResourceManager_GetById` to always return null — this test needs the opposite) and asserts `initialized == 1` and `anim_index >= 0`, i.e. the host branch actually ran to completion rather than silently no-op'ing. New meson target `entity-host-resource`, self-contained minimal link fixtures (no `g_tilemap`/`g_audio`/etc. needed since none of those paths fire for a plain `InitBase` call). `meson test -C build`: 32/32 including integration and the new test.

**2026-08-12 (entity-update-host-guard)**: Following the same construction-path trace, found `Entity::Update` (vtable[10], 0x405C40) — the per-tick animation-advance function `InputMgr`'s main loop calls on every live entity (`input/InputMgr.cpp:378`) — was still completely unguarded: it unconditionally reads `resource+0x20`'s `FrameData` array using `this->anim_index`, the exact same undersized-`SpriteResource` OOB read fixed in `InitBase`/`SetAnimState`/`SetFrame`, except this call site fires every game tick for every initialized entity rather than once at construction. Before this session's `InitBase` fix this was moot (nothing ever produced an `initialized == 1` host `Entity`); after it, any placed animated entity would hit this immediately once `INPUT_PlaceObject` starts constructing real entities. Confirmed via `core/HostMode3Bootstrap.cpp:129-144`'s own comment, which already documents the *same* crash for `g_game` specifically (worked around there by forcing `g_game->initialized = 0`, a one-object special case, not a general fix).

Did **not** port `Update`'s field reads to a host equivalent the way `SetAnimState`/`SetFrame` were — `FrameData::step_delay`/`wait_time`/`sound_fx_index`/`audio_delay`/`volume` (`+0x04`/`+0x08`/`+0x0C`/`+0x10`/`+0x14`, `shared/types.h`) have no verified mapping onto the `.dat` animation row's remaining tokens yet (see the still-open "Separate finding... FrameData field-name mismatch" note two entries above — `+0x14`/`+0x16` are already known-inconsistent between `shared/types.h`'s names and the parser's write order, and `Update` would need all five of these fields, not just the three `SetAnimState` needed). Guessing at this mapping would violate the "inspect the bytes" rule for exactly the fields already flagged as unresolved. Instead, added an `#ifndef _WIN32` guard matching `TileMap::ScrollRect`'s established convention (loud stderr rejection instead of a silent/crashing raw read), rate-limited to once per resource pointer (a static `std::unordered_set<const void*>`) since — unlike `ScrollRect`, called once per placement attempt — `Update` runs at tick rate and would otherwise spam the log continuously. The entity's frame holds at whatever `SetAnimState`'s host branch already set instead of advancing; this is a documented degradation (animation freezes on host until the field mapping is resolved), not a crash or corruption.

Extended `tests/entity_host_resource_test.cpp` to prove the guard actually fires rather than just compiling: calls `entity.Update()` twice on the same host entity and asserts `frame_index` is unchanged across both calls — if the guard didn't fire, the raw `field_at<FrameData*>(resource, 0x20)` read would almost certainly change (or crash on) `frame_index`, so an unchanged value is real evidence the early-return path ran, not an assumption. `meson test -C build`: 32/32 including integration; the new assertion's log line (`[HOST] Entity::Update: skipping animation advance...`) appears exactly once across the two calls, confirming the per-resource dedup works. **Next**: this is the fourth and (so far) last method found on the real construction/tick path with this landmine; before writing `INPUT_PlaceObject`, still need to guard `ResourceGameObject`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s own constructor bodies (`+0x522`/`+0x14D`/`+0x20` in `core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`, `+0x63A` in `game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`) using the same `SpriteMetadata` fields already resolved (`ee_replay_delay`, `name`, `tile_type`) — those are the next and, per the current trace, final layer before the dispatcher itself.

**2026-08-12 (state-63a-consumer-cluster + resourcegameobject-ctor-guard)**: Closed out the "next" item above. Tracing `state_63A`'s consumers (not just its producer) turned up a whole cluster, not the single `RESDATA_GameVehicle` constructor originally scoped: `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile` (`world/tilemap.cpp`, canonical), `RESDATA_IsRoadTile`/`IsWaterTile`/`IsTrackTile`/`IsSceneryTile`/`GetTileCategory` (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, all duplicating the same unconditional `+0x63A` read against their own value ranges), and `RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s constructor *and* its `StopSound` override (`game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`, two separate `+0x63A` reads, not one). All read the identical byte `SpriteMetadata::tile_type`/`has_tile_type` already covers. Added one shared accessor, `loco::assets::sprite_tile_type_byte(const void*, uint8_t*)` (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.{h,cpp}`) — false (untouched output) for a non-host resource or a host resource with no tile_type directive, true+byte otherwise — and a local `ResolveTileStateByte`/`ResolveVehicleTileType` wrapper in each of the two non-`resource_manager_sdl3.cpp` translation units (can't share one non-inline helper cleanly across TUs without a new shared header for a two-line function). `RESDATA_GetTileCategory` needed care: it's an unconditional loud stub (`assert(0)` fires regardless of the tile byte's value — the player/color-dependent path was never implemented) — the fix there only removes the OOB read on the way to that assert, it does not change whether the stub fires, per the project's "never return silent success from incomplete internal logic" rule.

Also guarded `ResourceGameObject`'s own constructor body (`core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`) — the last unguarded raw-offset site found on the `INPUT_PlaceObject` construction path — sourcing `+0x522`/`+0x14D`/`+0x20` from `ee_replay_delay`/`name`/`frame_sets` exactly as the `entity-initbase-host-safety` entry did for `Entity::InitBase`. One new wrinkle: `anim_index` can be `-1` here (unresolved by `SetAnimState`'s host branch), and unlike the original's harmless-ish `frames[-1]` OOB heap read, `frame_sets[-1]` on a `std::vector` is real corruption — the host branch checks `anim_index >= 0` first and skips the timer set otherwise, a documented behavioral divergence, not a guess.

Verified with two different techniques rather than one: (1) extended `tests/resource_manager_sdl3_test.cpp` to call `sprite_tile_type_byte()` directly against the already-real `0xc54`/`0xc0a` fixtures (expects raw bytes 9 and 0xb) and against `0x1012` (expects `false`, not a stale default enumerator) — a cheap, direct proof of the one piece of genuinely new logic; (2) did **not** add an equivalent construction-reachability test for `ResourceGameObject`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle` themselves — doing so would require a new test target linking `core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`/`game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp` against the real `resource_manager_sdl3.cpp` bridge (today's `persistence_fixtures.h`-based tests fake `ResourceManager_GetById` to always return null, same conflict hit earlier this session), which is real, nontrivial link-fixture work — so, honestly: these two constructors are **guarded, not reachability-confirmed**, same caveat status the `state_63A` consumers had before this entry's test. `meson test -C build`: 32/32 including integration.

**2026-08-12 (advisor-directed verification of the above)**: Before trusting the state-63A cluster fix, ran two independent checks the advisor specifically asked for, since the `TileTrackType` enum's semantic names (`LevelCrossingPathHorizontal`, etc.) look nothing like `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s independently-derived category comment ("water", "track/rail", "scenery") for the *same* numeric ranges — a red flag that either the two are just different vocabularies over identical bytes, or one of them is reading the wrong offset entirely. (1) **Disassembled `FUN_0044b4f0` directly** (not decompiler pseudocode) and grepped every store instruction: all 19 stores are `MOV byte ptr [EDI + 0x63a], <imm>` with `<imm>` exactly matching `TileTrackType`'s 19 values (1 through 0x13) — the producer provably writes the identical offset and value set `sprite_tile_type_byte()` reads, settling the "two vocabularies, one byte" question with hard evidence rather than an assumption. `stubs_impl.cpp:459-462`'s category labels are unverified naming from an earlier, separate pass and should not be read as contradicting `TileTrackType` — flagged in that file as a follow-up, not fixed (out of scope, no consumer depends on the label text). (2) **Measured actual coverage** with a throwaway host program (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.cpp` + `resource_archive.cpp` + `pe_string_table.cpp`, linked directly, not part of the build) that parses the real `curr` save's `SaveRegion` header properly — the entity-record-distribution measurement in the "state-63a-tile-track-type" entry above had used a header/stride guess (fixed `0x114`-byte offset to entities, `uint32_t resource_id`) that happened to still produce the right total record count (497) by coincidence but read every `resource_id` from the wrong byte offset; the real `SaveRegion` (`shared/types.h:222`) has a variable-length preview blob (`player_id * player_color` bytes, here 0) between the header and the entity array, and `EntityRecord::resource_id` (`input/PersistenceAdapter.h:64`) is `uint16_t`, not `uint32_t`. Fixed and re-measured: of the 272 type-3 records, all 272 resolve to a real archive resource (`ResourceManager_GetById` succeeds), but only **20 (7%)** have `SpriteMetadata::has_tile_type == true`. This corrects the "resolves the 272/497 records" framing in the two entries above — see those corrections. Net effect on the fix's correctness: **unchanged** — `sprite_tile_type_byte()` returning `false` for the other 252 is the intended, evidenced behavior (a resource whose `.dat` never carried a track keyword was never touched by `FUN_0044b4f0`), not a coverage gap to close. The real gap this surfaced is in prior session-log prose, not in the shipped guard code.

Three more advisor findings, all fixed: (3) the `Entity::Update` reachability test (`entity-update-host-guard` entry above) asserted `frame_index` unchanged across two calls, but the *original* also has early-return paths (single-frame no-loop animation, already-at-end-frame, mid-wait) that leave `frame_index` unchanged with no guard involved at all — for `0x1020` that assertion would coincidentally still pass with the guard deleted. Added `loco::host_test::entity_update_host_guard_hit_count()` (`core/Entity.h`/`core/GameObject.cpp`, `#ifndef _WIN32`-only, a plain counter incremented inside the guard) and asserted it advances by exactly 2 across the two calls — this actually distinguishes "the guard ran" from "the frame happened to hold anyway." (4) Fixed the stale `input/InputMgr.cpp:21-24` header comment claiming `INPUT_PlaceObject`/`INPUT_RemoveObject` are "NOT on the load/save path" — disproven earlier this session (`TileMap::FindObject` calls `INPUT_PlaceObject` directly, and `INPUT_LoadSaveFile` reaches `FindObject`); left uncorrected it would have sent the next reader back through the same investigation. (5) Ran the MinGW typecheck build (`ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`) across all five files this session's landmine sweep touched (`core/GameObject.cpp`, `core/BuildingMgrObjectGroup.cpp`, `game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`, `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, `world/tilemap.cpp`) specifically to catch the class of dangling-`#ifndef`/scoping bug the `anim_val` redeclaration earlier in this session's edits was an instance of. **Result: zero new failures from this session's changes.** Every error the typecheck build reports in these five files (and several others: `ui/EditWindow.cpp`, `town/TownTiles.cpp`) is pre-existing and unrelated — `invalid-offsetof` on non-standard-layout types in `BuildingMgrObjectGroup.h`/`ResdataGameVehicle.h`/`Building.h` (static_asserts none of this session's edits touched), `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`'s `operator_new(size_t)`/`operator_new(unsigned int)` becoming the same overload once `size_t` is 32-bit (a latent bug in code this session didn't write, only extended further down the same file), `world/tilemap.cpp`'s unrelated `uint`/`AssetMgr_LoadFileEx`/`AssetMgr_EnumFiles`/`Scroll` declarations, and `ui/EditWindow.cpp` calling `loco::assets::*` unconditionally outside any `#ifndef _WIN32` guard. None of this session's new `#ifndef _WIN32` blocks appear in the error output at all — they compile down to nothing under the Windows cross-compile, as intended. **2026-08-12 (state-63a-caller-confirmed)**: Closed the last open advisor item before `INPUT_PlaceObject` itself: re-ran the producer search properly, using `search_text` (regex over the whole binary's disassembly text) for `\b0x63a\]` instead of trusting the earlier `get_xrefs_to(0x44B4F0)`-only search that had (correctly, but narrowly) found no call site. 67 total hits across the binary — every reader and writer of this byte. Exactly one other writer besides the known classifier: `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_HandleEvent` (0x44B290), decompiled and confirmed to be a plain `*(this+0x63A) = 0` zero-init near its own top, executed unconditionally before it parses the `.dat` stream and calls `(**(code**)(*(int*)this+0xc))(stream)` — a virtual call through vtable slot `[3]`. Checked both functions' vtable-slot xrefs directly (`get_xrefs_to` on each function address, not inference): `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_HandleEvent` is slot `[4]` (data xref at `0x478368` = `0x478358+0x10`), the classifier is slot `[3]` (data xref at `0x478364` = `0x478358+0xC`) — same vtable, so HandleEvent's slot-3 virtual call on its own `this` resolves to the classifier. This **is** the classifier's real caller, closing the "producer found but caller unidentified" item that had stood since earlier this session (and, per that item's own note, since before the advisor's involvement — the "no confirmed caller" framing was carried forward across several entries without ever being re-checked with a wider search). Renamed `FUN_0044b4f0` → `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_ClassifyTileType`, added a Ghidra decompiler comment documenting the full caller chain, saved the database. No source-tree changes — this was pure RE confirmation, not a code fix — but it upgrades "very likely consistent with every predicate's naming" (this session's hedge on why 252/272 records defaulting to 0 is correct) to "confirmed": `state_63A`'s only two touch points in the entire binary are this classifier and HandleEvent's zero-init, so a resource whose `.dat` never used a track keyword has a real x86 `state_63A` of exactly 0, not an unknown/uninitialized value. Also surfaced (not fixed) a wider reader surface — see the resolved pending-list item for the full function list — none reachable from the current `INPUT_PlaceObject` construction-path trace, tracked for later.

**2026-08-12 (cursor-default-frame-set-fix + anim-index-resolution)**: Resolved the last open item before writing `INPUT_PlaceObject`: whether `ResourceGameObject`'s `frames[anim_index].wait_time >= 0` read (`+0x20`/`ChildWindow::heapBuffer`) is safe to translate to a host `frame_sets[anim_index]` read. Decompiled `UI_ChildWindow_Render`'s (0x424E00) `FrameData`-array-writing loop directly (not inferred from the `.dat` comment) and confirmed the `.dat` animation row's 5th numeric token writes `FrameData::wait_time` (+0x08) — matching the existing `AnimationFrameSet::restart_delay` field exactly. Traced `anim_index` resolution for a freshly-constructed `Entity(resource_id, -1, 0, 0)`: `GameObject::InitBase` (0x405900) force-sets `anim_index = -1`, then — because the passed index is negative — substitutes `resource->default_anim` (`RESDATA+0x1E`) and calls `SetAnimState` (0x405A50, reached via `StopSound`'s internal chain, a Ghidra vtable-slot-naming trap: slot [7] is labeled `StopSound` but its body itself calls slot [14] `SetAnimState`), which writes `anim_index` **only if** `0 <= default_anim < frame_set_count` — otherwise `anim_index` stays `-1`. This is a real, derived guard condition (not a guess): the host equivalent must check `default_anim` is in-range before indexing `frame_sets`, matching the original's failure mode (leaving the timer unset) instead of indexing `frame_sets[-1]` (UB on `std::vector`, unlike the original's harmless-ish OOB heap read).

Getting `default_anim` exposed a real, pre-existing bug: the `.dat` directive supplying it (`"cursor/default_frame_set"`) has two distinct real spellings in the shipped archive — 379 files use plain `"cursor_frame_set"` (already recognized), 198 files use `"cursor/default_frame_set"` (**not** recognized at all before this fix, since `tokens[0]` is compared by exact string equality and the two spellings differ) — meaning `SpriteMetadata::cursor_frame_set`/`cursor_frame` silently stayed at their zero defaults for those 198 resources, including the existing `0x341d` fixture. Fixed by accepting either spelling, matching `UI_ChildWindow_Render`'s own two `CRT_wcsstr` checks. A third real spelling, `"cursor_Frame_set"` (capital F, 8 files, e.g. `toybox\loadsave\options.dat`), is deliberately **not** recognized — `CRT_wcsstr` is case-sensitive, so the original parser doesn't recognize it either and falls through to `break` for those files; reproduced, not "fixed". Verified against `0x1020` (`scenery\bigfount.dat`, `"cursor/default_frame_set 12 0"` → `cursor_frame_set=12`, `cursor_frame=0`) with a non-zero-value fixture (a resource that happens to parse to `0` couldn't distinguish "parsed" from "still defaulted"). **Field-order correction**: the directive's *second* value (`cursor_frame` in the existing struct, not `cursor_frame_set`) is the one written to `+0x1E`/`default_anim` — using `cursor_frame_set` instead would silently select the wrong frame-set index. `meson test -C build`: 31/31 including integration.

**Separate finding, recorded but not fixed (out of scope for the placement chain)**: while tracing `UI_ChildWindow_Render`'s `FrameData`-write order, found the write positions for the `.dat` animation row's 9th/10th numeric tokens don't match `shared/types.h`'s `FrameData` field names — token 9 (mapped to `AnimationFrameSet::flip_x` in the existing host parser) writes to `+0x14`, which `shared/types.h` names `volume`, and token 10 (currently discarded as an unnamed `opaque_field` in the host parser) writes to `+0x16`, which `shared/types.h` names `flip_horizontal`/`is_connected`/`step_mode`. Either `shared/types.h`'s field names at `+0x14`/`+0x16` are wrong, or the host `.dat` parser's `AnimationFrameSet::flip_x` is reading the wrong column. Nothing on the `INPUT_PlaceObject`/tile-placement path reads `flip_x`, so this doesn't block that work — tracked here so the next reader doesn't inherit a silently-mismatched field name.

### input-place-object test strengthened; a real pointer-truncation bug found and fixed (2026-08-12, same day)

Advisor review of the `INPUT_PlaceObject` commit found two real gaps in `tests/input_place_object_test.cpp`, both closed before touching `TileMap::ScrollRect`: (1) `special_count` was never asserted, so the entire `sprite_leisure_destination_byte()` tail would have passed identically had it always returned false — resource `0x1020` is independently confirmed (`resource_manager_sdl3_test.cpp`) to carry `LeisureDestination 1`, so the test now asserts `special_count` advances by exactly 1; (2) three of the four `INPUT_PlaceObject` constructor branches (`GameVehicle`/`HelpPageNode`/`RESDATA_GameVehicle`) had never been reachability-tested — only `ResourceGameObject` (the type!=3 branch) had a real placement behind it, and 272/497 real save records are type==3, meaning the untested branches were exactly what `TileMap::ScrollRect` would hit first once its guard opens.

Added a second placement in the same test to exercise the type==3 branch. First candidate, resource `0xc0a` ("points", tile_type 0xb), failed with `initialized == 0` — same failure mode as `0x816` earlier in this chain: no decoded bitmap, a pure track-metadata descriptor. Real finding, not a test bug — tracked here rather than silently swapped away. Switched to `0xc54` ("depot top", tile_type 9, a building value → `GameVehicle` constructor), which does have a bitmap.

That placement immediately hit a real, previously-undiscovered defect: `RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s own constructor (`game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp:134`, unguarded, unlike the rest of the file) calls `RESDATA_IsRoadTile(int32_t)` directly on the untruncated host resource pointer whenever the already-resolved `tile_type` byte isn't 0x0C (train) or 0x0B (pedestrian) — the exact pointer-in-`int32_t` truncation defect found and only *tracked* (in `InputMgr.cpp`'s dispatch) earlier this session, now caught live because this was the first real placement to reach a type==3 constructor at all. Fixed the same way `InputMgr.cpp` guards its own copy: on host, check the already-resolved `tile_type` byte against `RESDATA_IsRoadTile`'s known value set (`{0x01,0x02,0x03,0x04}`, confirmed in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`) directly instead of re-deriving it through the truncated pointer; the original `int32_t`-signature call stays under `#else`/`_WIN32` unchanged.

Also moved `World_DeserializeMap` in `tests/persistence_fixtures.h` from an unconditional `LOUD_FIXTURE` to the same `PERSISTENCE_FIXTURES_REAL_RESOURCE_MANAGER`-conditional pattern as the RECT helpers: `~RESDATA_GameVehicle()` calls it unconditionally on teardown, and the real bridge (`shared/stubs_link001_batch4_network_world.cpp`) is already a `if (world == nullptr) return;` no-op — exactly what firing it with the fixture's `g_world == nullptr` produces, so the conditional real body is the existing verified behavior, not a new shortcut. Other persistence tests (which never construct a placeable `RESDATA_GameVehicle`) keep the loud fixture.

One chain-closing note advisor flagged: `ResourceManager::GetById`'s lazy-load path (`LoadStringTable` → `AddString`) is `ResourceManager_AddString`, whose sole caller is `RESDATA_ScriptedObject_AddChild`, which calls `HandleEvent` — the same `HandleEvent` that zero-inits `state_63A` before the classifier runs (see the `state-63a-caller-confirmed` entry). So every object `GetById` can return is one `HandleEvent` already zero-initialized before any classification happens — the state_63A-default-is-0 argument and the GetById-lazy-load path are the same chain, not two separate claims.

`meson test -C build`: 33/33 including integration and the strengthened `input-place-object` test (now exercising both dispatch branches and `special_count`). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: `game/ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`'s object still fails, but only on the same pre-existing `invalid-offsetof` class already tracked (`BuildingMgrObjectGroup.h`/`ResdataGameVehicle.h`'s non-standard-layout `offsetof` uses) — nothing new from this session's edit.

**Update (2026-08-12, tilemap-scrollrect-real-placement)**: `TileMap::ScrollRect`'s guard is relaxed and real placement works end to end. `TileMap::ReadTilePointer`/`WriteTileValue` used to round-trip a placed object through a raw `static_cast<int32_t>(reinterpret_cast<intptr_t>(ptr))` — measured, not assumed, to lose real host heap addresses on this 64-bit host (unlike the original 32-bit ABI, this isn't a rare ASLR edge case; it fires on ordinary `operator_new` addresses every time). Added a host-only tile-slot pointer registry (`TileMap::StoreTilePointer`, a `std::vector<void*>` + `std::unordered_map<void*,int32_t>` side table) as the sole sanctioned translation, and converted every raw-cast tile-pointer read across the file (`GetObjectAtEx`, `GetTileOrigin`/`GetTileOriginEx`, `ScrollRect`, `ScrollTo`, `UpdateViewport`, `FindNearestObject`) to go through it — these were forced fixes, not scope creep: they read/write the exact same tile storage `ScrollRect`/`FindObject` now actually populate, so leaving them on the old raw cast would silently misread every value once real placement started writing through the registry.

Retyped `ScrollRect`'s `target_building` parameter from `TileMapObject*` to `TileMapResource*` (disassembly of 0x4553E0 proved every occupancy-grid/span read in the function is against the raw resource, never a placed object) and implemented real host branches in both `ScrollRect` and `FindObject`: on host, `grid_width/height/depth`, `bitmap_grid_width/height`, and cell values come from `loco::assets::host_resource_manager().sprite_metadata()`'s `SpriteFootprint` (extended `resource_manager_sdl3.cpp`'s parser to actually store `bitmap_occupancy`'s cell values, not just its dims, plus a new `bitmap_occupancy_value()` accessor — verified against a standalone dump program before writing the corresponding test assertions in `resource_manager_sdl3_test.cpp`). Replaced the pointer-truncating `RESDATA_IsRoadTile(int32_t)` call inside `ScrollRect` with a direct tile_type-byte check on host — the same fix already applied in `InputMgr.cpp` and `ResdataGameVehicle.cpp`, now a third confirmed live instance of that defect class. Also fixed a real `result`-vs-`resource` occupancy-read bug in `FindObject` (was indexing a field `result` doesn't have) and a tile-address x/y swap in `ScrollRect`.

Corrected `TileMapResource`'s layout: `occupancy_grid` was undersized (`[9*7]`, should be `int8_t[0x333]` at +0x16E) and `span_map` (`uint8_t[0x75]` at +0x4A1) was missing entirely, folded into padding. Removed the previously-added, unevidenced `grid_width`/`grid_height`/`grid_depth`/`occupancy_grid` fields from `TileMapObject` — disassembly of both functions proves every such read is against the raw resource, never a placed object.

First real end-to-end run (via the GUI integration suite's mode-3 entry, which loads `curr.sav`) surfaced two more live defects, both never previously exercised because nothing had reached real placement before: `RESDATA_GameVehicle`'s constructor called a wrongly-declared free function `GameObject_StopSound` where the real target is virtual `Entity::StopSound(int)` (fixed all 4 call sites, removed the stale `extern`); and a genuine ODR type mismatch on `ATTR_0047f108` (`shared/stubs_impl.cpp` declared it `int ATTR_0047f108 = 0`, a scalar, against `world/tilemap.h`'s own `extern uint8_t ATTR_0047f108[8]` — the real type, confirmed via disassembly at 0x455342, `MOV AL, byte ptr [EAX + 0x47f108]` with EAX pre-masked to 0-7). The type mismatch was harmless while placement was rejected outright (the array was never indexed); real placement makes it live. **Deliberately left unfixed this session** (advisor-reviewed scope call): correcting the type wakes `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects`/`ProcessRect`'s DirectDraw presentation path for the first time in the project's history — dirty-tile detection goes from always-0 to sometimes-1 — which immediately SIGSEGVs on a null `g_cursor_surface` (confirmed via `coredumpctl debug` on a real core dump) and has at least two more raw-fixed-offset reads on `tobj->resource` in `ProcessRect` (`+4`/`GetResourceType`, `+0x20`/frame_table) — the same undersized-host-SpriteResource landmine class, needing the same `SpriteMetadata`/`AnimationFrameSet` host-accessor treatment already used elsewhere. That's the whole unported mode-3 DirectDraw pipeline, not a `ScrollRect` dependency (`g_primary_surface` is confirmed never assigned a real value on host, i.e. this pipeline has never run) — tracked here as its own follow-up rather than chased into this commit. Reverted the `ATTR_0047f108` type fix and its accompanying `g_cursor_surface` null-guard to confirm the isolation: with just that one hunk reverted, all 3 previously-failing integration tests go green again, with zero change to placement's own behavior (measured 4/497 both before and after the type fix — `FindObject`'s only read of `ATTR_0047f108` is a dirty-bit write, not placement state).

Added a real occupancy measurement rather than trusting a non-null `FindObject()` return: a resource with no physical/bitmap footprint (`grid_width/height == 0` and `orig_span == 0`) still constructs and returns an entity while writing zero tile-grid cells, so "placed" and "occupied a real slot" are different claims. Added `TileMap::last_find_object_cells_written` (host-only diagnostic field, incremented at both `WriteTileValue` fill sites in `FindObject`, reset at function entry) and a matching `occupied_count` in `INPUT_LoadSaveFile`. Measured result, from the real GUI integration run against `curr.sav`: **"placed 4 / 497 entity records (4 actually occupied a tile slot) into the real tilemap grid via TileMap::FindObject"** — all 4 placements that returned an entity also wrote real grid cells; the milestone this chain has been working toward ("wire one real record through `TileMap::FindObject` ... and assert one occupied tile slot") is now directly measured, not inferred.

`meson compile -C build` clean. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`, unchanged pass count from session start (isolated the `ATTR_0047f108`/`ScrollRect` change sets from each other before committing, per the paragraph above). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: zero new failures — the pre-existing `uint`-typedef/`EditWindow.cpp`/`CRT_malloc_zero`-redefinition failures are all on lines untouched by this session's diff (`git diff --stat` confirms a single additive hunk in `tilemap.h`, nowhere near any of the failing lines).

**Still open, tracked as its own follow-up, not a `ScrollRect` dependency**: the `ATTR_0047f108` type fix plus `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects`/`ProcessRect`'s DirectDraw presentation path (null `g_cursor_surface` guard, `tobj->resource+4`/`+0x20` raw-offset reads, `g_main_window`-derived `child_wnd` dereference, `DDRAW_PresentRect` host-safety) — the whole unported mode-3 rendering pipeline, exposed but not caused by this session's placement work.

Independently re-verified the reconstructed 22-slot vtable byte-for-byte against a fresh `read_bytes` of 0x477AE8 before trusting it, and independently re-verified three fabricated address claims (0x428DA0/0x428E40/0x4AA500) via `get_xrefs_to` (all zero, confirmed fabricated) before accepting the fix. `meson test -C build`: 33/33 including `--suite integration`. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical error set to the pre-change baseline (verified per-target via `git stash`/rebuild/`git stash pop`, since most of the 15 touched files are blocked from full-tree MinGW builds by an unrelated, pre-existing `ui/EditWindow.cpp` baseline failure that predates this session).

**2026-08-14 (directdraw-shim-phases-1-3)**: Started a real, API-compatible DirectDraw 6.0 shim library over SDL3 to replace three independent, mutually incompatible ad hoc `IDirectDraw4`/`IDirectDrawSurface4`/`DDSURFACEDESC`/`DDBLTFX` definitions in the tree (`stubs/ddraw.h`'s no-op stub, `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.h`'s non-virtual struct, `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp`'s local shadow) — see `NOTE-directx-sdk.md`'s DirectX-6.0-SDK determination and sourcing guardrail (real SDK headers may inform struct/interface shape; the original DirectDraw *implementation* must never be consulted or ported). Explicit scope decision: API compatibility only, not ABI/vtable-slot accuracy — the interfaces match real DirectDraw method names/signatures so decompiled call sites work unmodified, but method order isn't a slot-accurate COM replica, and every raw `vtbl[N]`-index call site in this tree must eventually resolve to a named call via RE evidence, not slot position.

Phase 1: fixed `platform/sdl3_types.h`'s `DDSURFACEDESC` (was `dwWidth` before `dwHeight`, no `ddsCaps`/`ddpfPixelFormat` — the opposite of the real DX6 SDK order), added the previously-missing `DDPIXELFORMAT`, completed `DDBLTFX`. Added a non-square (320×200) `GetSurfaceDesc` regression test — a square test surface can't catch a width/height transposition, which is the exact bug this struct already had once.

Phase 2+3: added `platform/ddraw_interfaces.h` declaring `IDirectDraw4`/`IDirectDrawSurface4`/`IDirectDrawPalette`/`IDirectDrawClipper` as pure-virtual C++ classes (same proven pattern as `stubs/dplay.h`'s `IDirectPlay4A` and `audio/AudioChannel.h`'s `AudioDirectSoundBuffer`/`Device`), moved the platform-neutral DirectDraw structs into that header since they have no SDL dependency, and had both `stubs/ddraw.h` (MinGW typecheck path) and `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.h` (host path) include it instead of each declaring their own copy. Renamed the existing working SDL3-backed concrete structs to `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface`/`Sdl3DirectDraw4`, made them real subclasses, added previously-absent methods (`BltFast`, `GetAttachedSurface`, `EnumSurfaces`, `GetCaps`, `WaitForVerticalBlank`), and converted `Blt`/`BltFast`/`Lock`/`Unlock` to take real `RECT*` (left/top/right/bottom) instead of an `SDL_Rect*`-shaped deviation, converting at the SDL boundary.

Making these interfaces genuinely virtual surfaced a real, would-be regression: every DirectDraw global in this tree (`g_ddraw`, `g_primary_surface`, `g_backbuffer`, `_g_primary_surface`, `g_cursor_back`) is permanently null on host today, and the old non-virtual no-op stub tolerated dispatch through a null `this` silently (no member touched); a real vtable means the same dispatch now reads a null vtable pointer and segfaults. Ran a full-tree sweep (dedicated Explore agent) for every cast-to-interface-then-call site not inside an `#ifdef _WIN32` guard, and fixed each with a `#ifndef _WIN32` null check immediately before the dispatch (not a permanent skip) so the exact original call still fires once a later phase wires a real device: `ui/GameWindow.cpp` (`CreateSurface`, `Restore`, two `Blt` sites — currently dead on host since `GameWindow::create/show/hide` are only invoked from the `#else`/real-Windows branch of `CGWND_InitAllSubsystems`, but a genuine future-facing fix, not just defensive noise), `native/DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c` and `town/TownTiles.cpp`'s `Town_CheckOccupiedEx` (both confirmed *live* — reachable unconditionally from `game/BuildingMgr.cpp`/UIPANEL drawing code today). Also replaced `ui/GameWindow.cpp`'s local byte-offset-annotated `DDSurfaceDesc` shadow struct with the canonical `DDSURFACEDESC`, since `CreateSurface`'s real parameter type now enforces it (previously silently tolerated via `-fpermissive` against a `void*` parameter) — its field layout already matched the shadow's own byte-verified-against-disassembly comment.

`meson test -C build`: 32/34 (2 pre-existing, unrelated network-sandbox failures, confirmed via `git stash` bisection) + integration suite green, including a new `sdl3-ddraw-surfacedesc` test. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: 53/54 FAILED-set, improved from the 54/54 baseline (`town/TownTiles.cpp.obj` now compiles), zero new failures. Remaining phases before `ATTR_0047f108` can be un-reverted: Phase 4 (minimal `IDirectDrawPalette`/`IDirectDrawClipper`), Phase 5 (wire the real global singletons), Phase 6 (migrate `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` and the raw-`vtbl[N]` call sites in `native/ddraw_surface_ops.c`/`native/DDRAW_GetSurfaceWidthHeight.c`/`native/ddraw_clippers.c`/`input/Cursor_internal.h` onto the canonical interface).

Implemented a Lock/Unlock-boundary RGB565↔XRGB8888 adapter entirely inside `graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp` (zero changes to `native/DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c`/`town/TownTiles.cpp`, both confirmed live 16bpp-shaped consumers), re-deriving the RGB565 constants from `DDRAW_GetSurface`'s own decompiled branch rather than guessing; found and fixed a real latent bug in the process — `g_surface_red_mask`/`g_surface_blue_mask` had no defining declaration anywhere in the tree, tolerated only by the main binary's unresolved-symbols-ignore linker flag. Migrated `ui/UIPANEL_Surface.cpp` and `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp` off their own two *separate* local shadow DirectDraw interfaces onto the canonical `platform/ddraw_interfaces.h` types (both committed). While starting `ui/UIPANEL.cpp`'s migration, found a serious new blocker and stopped there per advisor review: `UIPANEL_BeginPaint`'s raw `GetDC` dispatch retries 1000× then calls `ExitProcess(1)`, and `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::GetDC` is a permanent no-op that always fails — so wiring `g_primary_surface` non-null today would make the game reliably kill itself ~10 seconds after the first real `UIPANEL_BeginPaint` caller (e.g. `BuildingPanel`) runs. Reverted the partial, uncommitted `ui/UIPANEL.cpp` edits (an include and an unrelated but real independent bug fix — `DDBLT_WAIT` was locally defined as `0x10`, really `DDBLT_ASYNC`; canonical value is `0x01000000`, currently harmless since `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::Blt` ignores its `flags` param) rather than leave the file half-migrated with zero dispatch sites actually converted. A real `GetDC` implementation is now a hard prerequisite for wiring the surfaces, alongside the already-resolved pixel-format issue. `meson test -C build`: 34/34 (2 pre-existing unrelated failures) after each of the two committed file changes. `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical 53/53 FAILED set throughout.
