**2026-08-14 (attr-0047f108-fourth-attempt-mode3-still-frozen)**: User ran the fresh compile manually and reported the exact `BUG-mode-3-render-freeze.md` symptom on a real singleplayer Accept click. Reproduced independently via the GUI sandbox (not just taking the report on faith): `test_singleplayer_accept_reaches_mode3_without_crashing`'s own `singleplayer-mode3-stable.png` showed the unchanged main-menu image 2s after `mode_changed→3`. Root-caused to `ATTR_0047f108` (full detail in the Priority-1 note above) — un-reverted the type fix a fourth time, now for real: all three previously-blocking crashes (`g_cursor_surface`, `g_tooltip_mgr`, `Entity::Draw`'s `UIPANEL_Blit` gap) were already resolved/guarded from prior sessions, but two *new*, previously-unreached sibling null-`resource` dereferences inside `TileMap::ProcessRect` itself surfaced immediately (`+4` res_type read, `+0x20` frame-table read) — each caught via `coredumpctl`/gdb backtrace against a real crash, not guessed, and fixed with the same null-guard pattern `Entity::Draw` already uses for the identical landmine class. `meson test -C build`: 34/36 (unchanged baseline) including `--suite integration`; `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: line-shifted-only diff, zero new errors (`git stash` bisection).

**The user's reported freeze is still not fixed.** Verified this precisely rather than assuming success from "no crash": pixel-diffed the mode-3-stable screenshot before/after with `imagemagick compare` and sampled raw pixel values — differences are ±1-per-channel RGB565 quantization noise, not new content (e.g. `srgb(87,128,49)`→`srgb(87,129,49)`), proving `TileMap_Lock/UnlockPrimarySurface` now genuinely execute but write back the exact same pixels. Read all of `TileMap::ProcessRect` end to end to find why: its only two draw-shaped calls are `Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected` (guarded-skip — every real host resource hits this) and `DDRAW_DispatchToSubObjects` (unimplemented stub) — there is no separate terrain/background blit anywhere in the function. So today's fix is real, tested, necessary progress (dirty detection went from permanently-0 to genuinely live, two real crashes eliminated, zero regressions) but not sufficient — nothing can appear on screen until Priority 3's `UIPANEL_Blit`/host-`SpriteResource` mapping (bullet below) is implemented for real. Advisor consulted before declaring the flip safe to attempt (two of three prior blockers already resolved, third already a no-crash guard) and again before concluding scope — did not push further into Priority 3's larger, unscoped surface-mapping work without checking in first.

**2026-08-14 (priority3-scoping)**: Scoped Priority 3 (the `UIPANEL_Blit`/host-`SpriteResource` mapping gap left open above) via fresh Ghidra decompilation, at the user's request — full detail folded into the Priority-1 bullet's re-scoped sub-notes above. Three findings, each verified rather than inferred:
  1. **The blit adapter is much smaller than previously scoped.** `UIPANEL_Blit`'s `mode==1` branch (the one `Entity::Draw` needs) is one `IDirectDrawSurface4::Blt()` call through `ddraw_surf` — not the software tile-cache/palette/pitch path the earlier "adapter/repack for SpriteResource" framing implied (that's `mode==0`, used only by `town/TownTiles.cpp`'s Town-tile methods). And `resource+0x10`'s "recover the original RESDATA layout" framing was moot: `Entity::resource` is already the host `SpriteResource*` bridge (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.cpp:587`), so there's no x86 UIPANEL_Surface to find — this is new host-only adapter work (same shape as the existing `DDRAW_LoadBmpToSurface`), not an RE excavation.
  2. **`FrameData::flip_horizontal`'s host source — resolved same day.** Read `UI_ChildWindow_Render`'s (0x424E00) real write sequence directly rather than guessing a same-position-in-a-shorter-list mapping (which would have repeated the exact `TileTrackType`/"depot top" ordering mistake from 2026-08-12): the 9th `.dat` token maps to `FrameData::volume` (+0x14), not `AnimationFrameSet`'s previously-misnamed `flip_x`, and the 10th (previously-discarded "opaque_field") token is the real `flip_horizontal` (+0x16), confirmed via disassembly of both extractor call sites (0x4252CE/0x4252D5 and 0x4252DA/0x4252E1/0x4252ED). Fixed in `resources/resource_manager_sdl3.h`/`.cpp`: renamed `flip_x`→`volume`, added a real `flip_horizontal` field sourced from the 10th token. `meson test -C build resource-manager-sdl3 entity-host-resource input-place-object`: all pass; confirmed `flip_x` had zero other tree-wide usages before renaming.
  3. **New finding, likely the bigger of the two blockers**: decompiled the *original* `TileMap::ProcessRect` (0x456700) directly and confirmed (not inferred from the host port alone) that its two `Entity`-vtable draw calls are the *only* rendering path for anything visible in mode 3 — no separate terrain/backdrop blit exists anywhere, and `get_xrefs_to` shows `ProcessRect` has exactly one caller in the whole binary. So grass/roads/scenery render through the same per-tile-object mechanism as decorative placed objects, meaning `input/PersistenceAdapter.h`'s `host_placement_available()` gate (default false, only 4/497 records placed in the observed save) blocks the town's appearance at least as much as the blit gap does — not scoped further this pass. No implementation attempted; `meson test`/`ninja` unaffected (no code changes this entry, PROGRESS.md only).

Added `tests/uipanel_surface_construction_test.sh` as a permanent regression guard, since this tree's `-Wl,--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all` link flag would otherwise let a reintroduced stale free-function reference "build clean" and only crash at runtime. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 including `--suite integration` (2 pre-existing unrelated failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set (53/53) via `git stash` diff. The blit-adapter work itself (finding 1 above) was not attempted this entry.

**2026-08-14 (blit-adapter-wired-placement-coverage-confirmed-blocking)**: User asked to validate town rendering via the GUI sandbox before declaring done; when that showed the freeze unchanged, asked to continue and implement the blit adapter (Priority 3 finding 1) directly. Implemented `loco::assets::get_or_create_uipanel_surface()` (`resources/sprite_uipanel_adapter.cpp`, new file): lazily builds and caches one `UIPANEL_Surface` per host `SpriteResource` (`mode=1`, `ddraw_surf` = `SDL3_WrapSdlSurfaceAsDirectDraw()` wrapping the resource's already-decoded `SDL_Surface*`, matching `DDRAW_LoadBmpToSurface`'s existing pattern). Rewired `Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected` (`core/GameObject.cpp`) to route host `SpriteResource`s through this adapter and `SpriteMetadata::frame_sets[anim_index].flip_horizontal`/`is_connected` instead of the guarded skip, unifying the host/original branches into one shared blit-call tail instead of duplicating it. **Deliberately split `UIPANEL_Surface`'s constructor/destructor/copy-constructor out of `graphics/LOCOBITMAP.cpp` into a new minimal file, `graphics/UIPANEL_Surface_lifecycle.cpp`**: several narrow unit tests (`entity_host_resource_test`, `inputmgr_canonical_test`, `input_world_test`, `input_place_object_test`) link `core/GameObject.cpp.o` under strict linking (no `--unresolved-symbols=ignore-all`), and pulling in all of `LOCOBITMAP.cpp` for just the constructor cascaded into ~35 additional undefined symbols (PostcardAlbum's Win32/sprite/pixel-cache dependency graph) — confirmed empirically before deciding to split, not guessed. Added local test fixtures (`tests/persistence_fixtures.h`, `tests/entity_host_resource_test.cpp`) for the handful of symbols the lean lifecycle file still needs (`g_ref_count`, `g_ddraw`, `SDL3_WrapSdlSurfaceAsDirectDraw` stub), following this codebase's existing "local fixture over pulling in the whole stub file" precedent (`tests/host_mode3_bootstrap_test.cpp`'s `operator_new`/`GLOBAL_free`). Along the way found and fixed one more `OutputDebugStringA` linkage inconsistency (this codebase already has two incompatible conventions for it — `extern "C"` in `LOCOBITMAP.cpp`, correctly matching the real Win32 ABI; plain C++ linkage in `shared/stubs_impl.cpp`, pre-existing and out of scope to reconcile today — matched the new lifecycle file's fixtures to the former since that's what it links against).

**Verified via the wayland-gui-sandbox, not just build success**: the `[HOST] Entity::Draw: skipping blit` log line is gone (confirming the new code path is genuinely reached), and `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects` now logs "4000 dirty tile(s) detected" (up from the guarded-zero state). But `imagemagick compare` between the pre- and post-change `singleplayer-mode3-stable.png` screenshots shows **zero pixel difference (AE=0)** — the visible freeze is still fully present. Root-caused why: read `TileMap::ProcessRect` end to end again with this specific question in mind, and confirmed `Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected` are called **only** when `ReadTilePointer()` returns a non-null object for that tile slot — there is no separate "always draw the grass/terrain" path. The startup log's own line makes the scale concrete: `seed_fresh_world: seeded 497 entity + 1 vehicle records ... placement coverage: 0 placed / 497 carried` and `INPUT_LoadSaveFile: 'curr' placed 4 / 497 entity records ... into the real tilemap grid`. With 4 of 497 tile slots occupied, `ProcessRect` has almost nothing to iterate over regardless of how correct the blit adapter is — this **confirms** (upgraded from "likely," per the previous scoping entry's finding 3) that `input/PersistenceAdapter.h`'s `host_placement_available()` placement-coverage gate is the actual blocker for any visible change, not a parallel or lesser one. The blit adapter itself is real, tested, necessary, and unblocks Priority 3 finding 1 for whenever placement coverage is fixed — but is not sufficient on its own, exactly as the prior scoping pass predicted. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 including `--suite integration` (2 pre-existing unrelated network-namespace failures). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: unaffected (all touched files are `#ifndef _WIN32`-guarded or host-only). Placement-coverage work itself was not attempted this entry — flagged to the user as the next real blocker rather than silently expanding scope into it.

**2026-08-14 (input-placeobject-recovered-440-of-497)**: User set `/goal recover INPUT_PlaceObject` to attack the placement-coverage gate identified as the actual blocker above. Found and fixed three independent, real bugs using diagnostic-instrumentation-then-remove (temporary `[DIAG]` `fprintf`s at each component boundary, removed once root-caused; verified empty via `grep -rn "\[DIAG]"` before considering any fix done):

1. **Frame-set parser never activated** (`resources/resource_manager_sdl3.cpp`, `parse_sprite_metadata`). The parser gated animation-row recognition on a fictional `"animation"` keyword line via an `in_animation` boolean; that keyword never appears in any real `.dat` file (confirmed against raw dumps of `track/strhlf-v.dat` and `track/switch-h.dat`). `frame_sets` stayed permanently empty regardless of the correctly-parsed `frame_set_count`, so every placed entity with a declared frame set failed `Entity::SetAnimState`'s bounds check and was torn down as "uninitialized." Fixed by replacing the boolean with a `frame_set_rows_remaining` countdown seeded from `frame_set_count`, matching the existing `physical_rows_remaining`/`bitmap_rows_remaining` pattern already used for the sibling occupancy sections.
2. **Pointer-truncation crash in two `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile` call sites** (`input/InputMgr.cpp`: `INPUT_LoadSaveFile`'s building-tile classification, and `entity_matches()` case 3 used by `INPUT_FindObjectAt`). `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile`'s real x86 ABI signature is `uint8_t __fastcall(int32_t)` — a genuine pointer-in-int32_t hazard on a 64-bit host heap. Once fix 1 let placement proceed past a handful of records, both sites SIGSEGV'd inside `is_host_sprite_resource` on a reconstructed garbage pointer (`coredumpctl` backtraces confirmed the exact call chain). `INPUT_PlaceObject`'s own dispatch already had the correct workaround — call `loco::assets::sprite_tile_type_byte()` directly on the untruncated pointer instead of going through `RESDATA_IsBuildingTile` at all — applied the same pattern to both remaining call sites, each `#ifndef _WIN32`-guarded with the original int32_t-truncating call preserved for the Windows/original path.
3. **`TileMapObject`/`Entity` layout mismatch** (`world/tilemap.cpp`, `TileMap::ProcessRect`). After fix 2, a third SIGSEGV appeared reading `resource` through the raw x86-offset-mirror `TileMapObject` struct (`tobj->resource` at the mirrored `+0x40`), which does not match the real host object's actual C++ layout (a proper `Entity`-derived class with a vtable and 64-bit members) — this read garbage, not the real resource pointer. `obj` is genuinely an `Entity*` (the adjacent `Draw()` call in the same function already treats it as one). Fixed both the res_type-read block and the sibling is_connected-read block to go through `static_cast<Entity*>(obj)->resource`, and corrected the `#ifndef _WIN32`/`#else` guard placement so the raw offset read only runs on the original/Windows path.

**Result**: placement coverage went from 4/497 to **440/497** (88.5%); the remaining 57 are confirmed legitimate `TileMap::ScrollRect` collision rejections (occupied-cell conflicts), not `INPUT_PlaceObject` defects — verified by reading each rejection's log reason, not assumed. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated network-namespace failures as before this entry). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: byte-identical FAILED set vs. the pre-existing baseline (all changed files are `#ifndef _WIN32`-guarded or match the original path exactly). Sandbox-verified after cleanup that the 440/497 count persists with all diagnostics removed.

**Re-checked town rendering with a fresh pixel-diff (2026-08-14, after the above fixes)**: still frozen, confirmed by `imagemagick compare`, not eyeballing. Diffed a genuine pre-fix run's screenshot (`gui-20260814T073315Z-31183`, logged `placed 4 / 497`) against this entry's post-fix cleanup-verification run (`gui-20260814T160022Z-260116`, logged `placed 440 / 497`): raw `-metric AE` reports ~2.3e8 differing pixels, but `-metric MAE` is only 224/65535 (~0.34%) and `-metric AE -fuzz 2%` (and `-fuzz 10%`) both report **0** — i.e. the only difference is exactly the already-documented ±1-per-channel RGB565 round-trip quantization noise (see this file's `ATTR_0047f108` finding), not new visible content. Both screenshots show the same mode-2 `EditWindow` name-entry/player-select screen (LEGO LOCO title, "test" name field, checkmark button), confirmed by `events.jsonl` to have been captured after `mode_changed` fired `2→1→3` at 842ms, well before shutdown. So fixing `INPUT_PlaceObject` (this entry) resolved the placement-coverage gate as its own real defect — 440/497 entities now genuinely occupy tile slots and are eligible for `TileMap::ProcessRect`'s draw calls — but did **not** by itself resolve the user's original "screen freezes on Accept" report. The primary-surface-pointer-mismatch hypothesis is already ruled out (see the `blit-adapter-wired-placement-coverage-confirmed-blocking` entry's `Blt()`/`SDL3_PresentPrimarySurface` instrumentation: hr=0 on real tile-sized rects, consistent surface pointers). Not scoped or attempted as part of the `INPUT_PlaceObject` goal above — flagged as the next real blocker for visible mode-3 rendering, now narrower: placement and blit are both real and wired, yet nothing new reaches the screen, which points at either the dirty-rect/viewport bounds actually drawn into, or something else between `ProcessRect`'s draw calls and the presented texture.

**2026-08-14 (mode3-town-now-visibly-rendering): root cause found and fixed — `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface::Lock`/`Unlock` clobbered every Blt() that ran while the surface was "locked."** Traced the call chain from the still-frozen screen above: `TileMap::InvalidateDirtyRects` (`world/tilemap.cpp`) locks the primary surface, calls `ProcessRect` (which draws all 440 placed entities via `Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected` → `UIPANEL_Blit`'s hardware path → `Blt()`), then unlocks. `Blt()` (`graphics/sdl3_ddraw.cpp`) draws GPU-side straight onto the destination's `SDL_Texture`. But `Lock()` separately reads that texture back into a CPU-side RGB565 `cpu_surface` (needed so `native/DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect.c`/`Town_CheckOccupiedEx` see real 16bpp pixels), and `Unlock()` unconditionally pushed that **stale, pre-Blt snapshot** back over the texture via `SDL_UpdateTexture` — erasing every entity `ProcessRect` had just drawn, in the same frame, before it could ever be presented. This is why `Blt()` returning `hr=0` and consistent primary-surface pointers (verified earlier this session) still produced zero visible change.

Checked against the assembly before changing anything, per this project's evidence rule: decompiled `TileMap_InvalidateDirtyRects` (0x456150) confirms the *original* nests the identical pattern — Lock, then `TileMap_ProcessRect` (which itself calls the hardware-Blt path), then Unlock, all on `g_primary_surface`. So the Lock/Blt/Unlock nesting itself is not a reconstruction ordering bug; real DirectDraw on period hardware evidently tolerated (or the primary surface's Lock() was a raw pointer into the same backing memory `Blt()` wrote to, making the "clobber" impossible on real hardware). The bug is specific to this host shim's destructive CPU-round-trip design, not the control flow.

**Fix** (`graphics/sdl3_ddraw.h`/`.cpp`, `Sdl3DirectDrawSurface`): added a `lock_snapshot` member that duplicates `cpu_surface`'s content right when `Lock()` produces it. `Unlock()` now re-reads the texture's *current* state (which reflects any `Blt()` calls that ran while locked), diffs the caller's `cpu_surface` against `lock_snapshot` pixel-by-pixel, and writes back only the pixels the caller actually changed — everything else keeps whatever the texture holds right now. This preserves both real use cases: direct CPU pixel writers (`DDRAW_DimSurfaceRect`, `Town_CheckOccupiedEx`) still get their edits applied, and `Blt()` calls nested inside a lock window are no longer erased.

**Verified visually, not just by log lines**: fresh GUI-sandbox screenshot (`gui-20260814T162100Z-267728`) now shows real new content — train tracks, multiple new buildings, and structures overlaid on the menu backdrop that were never there before. Pixel-diffed against the still-frozen pre-fix screenshot (`gui-20260814T160022Z-260116`): `-metric MAE` jumped from 0.34% (pure ±1-per-channel RGB565 quantization noise, per the entry above) to **1.79%**, and `-metric AE -fuzz 2%` — which showed exactly 0 before this fix — now reports a large, real difference. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated network-namespace failures). `meson test -C build --suite integration`: 1/1. This closes the mode-3 visual-freeze investigation that started this whole thread (`Before declaring done, validate using the gui sandbox that we can get to town rendering`) for real, pixel-verified, not just "pipeline runs without crashing."

**Correction/follow-up, same day: user confirmed only partial fix — some buildings/rails draw, but others are still missing, and the town's base terrain/background is completely absent.** Investigated both.

*Second real bug found and fixed (does not explain the remaining symptom, but is a genuine landmine)*: `world/tilemap.cpp`'s `g_player_id`/`g_player_color` globals (0x4AAD46/0x4AAD48) are used throughout the file's tile-occupancy-bit indexing (`InvalidateRect`, `ScrollTo`, `InvalidateDirtyRects`, `ProcessRect`, `FindObject`) and grid bounds checks (`ProcessObjectTimer`) — but the file's own pre-existing comments on these externs already said "(TileMap.tile_count_x)" / "(TileMap.tile_count_y)": these addresses are really `TileMap`'s own `tile_count_x`/`tile_count_y` member fields (confirmed via Ghidra disassembly of `GAMESTATE_SendScenarioSelect`, 0x40AD20, which packs the *same* two adjacent words into a network message right alongside genuine `g_player_id`/`g_player_color` fields — in the original binary these fields' storage is reused/aliased for both roles). `g_player_id` defaults to 0 outside a multiplayer session, which collapsed the occupancy-bit formula `stride*y+x` to just `x` — aliasing every row onto the same bit. Fixed by reading `this->tile_count_x`/`tile_count_y` directly at all 9 call sites instead of the wrongly-shared globals (which keep their real meaning for their genuine network-identity callers elsewhere). `meson test -C build`: 35/37. **Verified this fix has zero visible effect** on the still-partial render (pixel-diff MAE=0, byte-identical screenshot before/after) — real, evidenced, worth keeping, but not the cause of the remaining symptom.

*Missing building/rail types*: root cause not yet found. Ruled out sprite/bitmap load failure via temporary diagnostic (0 hits across all 440 placed entities — every placed object successfully resolves a resource and decodes a bitmap). Ruled out the occupancy-bit aliasing above. Not yet checked: whether affected objects are drawn but off-screen (a 4000-tile/80×50 viewport at 1280×800 vs. a town likely larger than one screen — normal scrolling would explain much of this, not a bug), or a per-layer/per-type drawing gap in `ProcessRect`'s layer loop.

*Background/terrain completely missing*: traced as far as confirming **no code anywhere in this tree loads or draws a per-town base terrain/background bitmap** — `TileMap::ProcessRect`'s only two draw-shaped calls (`Entity::Draw`/`DrawConnected`, gated on `obj != NULL`) never fire for empty tile slots, confirmed identical in the original's own decompiled 0x456700, so grass/terrain was never meant to come from per-tile objects. Checked `INPUT_LoadSaveFile`'s real decompile (0x41D5C0): the `.sav` header carries no background/map bitmap reference. `Town::init_postcard_sprites`' `background_resource`/`background_surface` (+0x64C/+0x650) is a red herring — that's the postcard-mailing UI's background art, unrelated to the town canvas. Real shipped `.sav` files (`SAVEGAME/Wildwest.sav` et al.) place only a few hundred decorative objects onto grid sizes far larger than that, meaning the base terrain must come from a separate, currently-unrecovered mechanism (most likely a per-town static bitmap or a `mode==0` software tile-cache initial paint — `UIPANEL_Blit`'s `DrawTile`/`InitTileCache` flag-dispatch path, never exercised on host since every host sprite adapter hardcodes `mode=1`). **This is new, substantial RE scope — not a quick fix** — needs the real function(s) that load/select and initially paint a town's canvas identified before any fix is attempted.

### Two more real coverage bugs against `/goal >90% match on wildwest-minimal.png` (2026-08-14, same thread)

Background/terrain turned out to already be solved by the time this thread resumed (a separate `resources/town_backdrop_adapter.cpp` loads `.sav`'s `SaveRegion::name` as `backdrop/<name>.bmp` and blits it as the base layer — see that file's own header comment) — the "missing buildings/rails" symptom was the real remaining gap. Found and fixed two more real bugs, both documented in full under the new Priority-1 checklist item above (`Two real render-coverage bugs found and fixed`): (1) `TileMap::Init()` never synced `width`/`height`/`viewport_rect` to the free-standing globals `g_world_width`/`g_world_height`/`g_viewport_rect_*` that `TileMap_FreeDirtyRects` actually reads, so every dirty rect outside the hardcoded 800×600 default (`shared/link_stubs.cpp`) was silently dropped; (2) `TileMap::FindObject`'s active-layer-byte updates used an unsigned comparison against a sentinel (`0xFF`) that only behaves as "less than everything" under a signed comparison, matching the real x86's `CMP+JL` — multi-layer buildings like the clocktower could never advance past their first (wrong) layer.

Measured with a rigorous, sanity-checked coverage metric instead of eyeballing screenshots: content-mask isolation against the real `arrid.bmp` backdrop (`magick <img> arrid.bmp -compose difference -composite -threshold 5% -colorspace Gray -alpha off`), then `coverage = |ref_mask ∩ shot_mask| / |ref_mask|` via `-compose darken`. Result: **84.21%** (`ref_content_px=225183`, `intersect_px=189636`), reproducibly, against `tests/reference/wildwest-minimal.png`. Below the `/goal`'s 90% target. `meson test -C build`: 35/37 (2 pre-existing unrelated netns failures, unchanged baseline). `ninja -C build-mingw -k 0`: no new failures (pre-existing `uint`/`AssetMgr_LoadFileEx`/`Scroll`-type errors at lines untouched by this session).

Chased a third hypothesis (`ScrollRect`'s displaceable-object check needs `TileMapObject::is_moving = 1` set at construction) far enough to write a fix, then found via diagnostic instrumentation that the byte read back as 0 later in the same constructor chain — reverted per advisor review rather than ship a `reinterpret_cast`-based raw-offset write (a first pass mistakenly attributed the read-back-as-0 to colliding with `ResourceGameObject::group_active`; that attribution turned out wrong, see below). Recorded as a dead end rather than retried blindly.

Finished the investigation properly rather than stopping at "reverted, unexplained": a fresh missing-content overlay (ref-only mask composited onto the real screenshot) showed the remaining ~15.8% gap concentrated in building/track-junction-sized regions, not scattered scenery. Cross-referencing a temporary rejection-tile diagnostic (`TileMap::FindObject`, logging every rejected record's `resource_id`/`tile_x`/`tile_y`) against those regions' pixel bounding boxes (direct `/16` tile conversion — this build's `TileMap::Init` produces a 1280×1024 world exactly matching the screen, so there's no additional viewport offset) confirmed **all 57 `ScrollRect` rejections land exactly inside the missing-content regions**, and their total footprint area matches the measured missing-pixel count almost exactly. This is not a drawing-path bug: it's placement rejection. Resolved the resource IDs to real names (`PeStringTable` dump) — a genuine mix of track switches/turnouts, buildings (school, gas station, shops), scenery, and paths, matching the user's original report.

Root-caused *why* `ScrollRect`'s displaceable check never passes, and it's a bigger and more serious finding than "a flag is never set": built a standalone `sizeof`/`offsetof` probe against the real project headers and proved `TileMapObject` — the hand-offset x86 mirror struct that `ScrollRect`/`is_moving`/`tile_x`/`tile_y`/`occupancy_*`/`build_*` are all read through via `reinterpret_cast`, tree-wide (~54 call sites) — has every field *after* its `resource` pointer member shifted 4 bytes from where its own field comments claim, because that pointer is 8 bytes on this 64-bit host, not the 4 the hardcoded padding arrays assume (`offsetof(TileMapObject, is_moving) == 196`, not the documented 192; `sizeof(Entity) == 168`, so this is read past the end of a plain `Entity` and lands on an unrelated real member of whichever larger derived class actually got allocated there). This also disproves the earlier "collides with `group_active`" theory from this same thread (`offsetof(ResourceGameObject, group_active) == 264`, not 196 — no collision; the earlier diagnostic write landed somewhere else, likely `member_objects`/`linked_objects`, which the constructor's own later field-inits then clobbered back to 0). This is a live, silent memory-corruption bug affecting every write through this mirror, not just an unreliable read of `is_moving` — see the new Priority-1 checklist item for the full writeup and the ~54-call-site blast radius. Fixing it for real needs a dedicated model-unification pass (give these fields a real canonical home in the `Entity`/`GameObject` hierarchy and convert every call site off the mirror struct), scoped but deliberately not attempted this session, per advisor review. This is why coverage stays at **84.21%**, not 90%, at the end of this thread.
